    - Not taken: (2) a per-service setting on mmm, which drops the header on all of mmm's names, ur.io and its pages included; (3) relying on `frame-ancestors` overriding the header, which leaves a header that says the opposite of the policy.
    - Raised by the build (OPS-M4-01; the build's ledger, QD-678).

487. **A VPN component's `narrowing_overdue` alarm backs off to daily while its condition lasts, and comes again at once for each newly overdue entry (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-11; it follows owner decision 216).**
    - Question: under owner decision 216 a narrowing statement the posture cannot check never makes the component pending, and `bridge_alarm` `narrowing_overdue` came at 10 minutes and again every 10 minutes; on a device that can never compile the condition lasts for good, so the alarm repeated forever and trained people to ignore it.
    - Answer: a VPN component raises `narrowing_overdue` when a narrowing entry has waited `NarrowingOverdueTimeout` (10 min) for a compile, then again at doubling intervals (20 min, 40 min and so on) up to once a day while the condition lasts, and at once again whenever a further narrowing entry becomes overdue; the condition (the oldest overdue position and its age) stays visible in the posture's status throughout, and the first compile that runs clears it. Owner decision 216's accepted cost is unchanged (§5.4's alarm reasons, §18.10's memory row).
    - Cost: the design details' staleness wording and Fwd.tla's alarm clause, and the posture's alarm timer.
    - Not taken: (2) every 10 minutes, as built, each app deciding how to show it; (3) once per episode, kept in status until a compile clears it.
    - Raised by the orchestrator's review of DESIGN-DETAILS.md section 4 (the build's ledger, QD-696).

488. **The lb exposes `Date` to cross-origin fetches, so a page Worker signs its DPoP `iat` by api's clock as every other leg does (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-13; it follows owner decision 316).**
    - Question: owner decision 316 has a sidecar sign its DPoP `iat` by api's clock, read from the Date header of its api answers, but the page Worker calls api cross-origin, a browser hides every response header neither CORS-safelisted nor exposed, and the lb's `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` did not name Date, so a page Worker signed by the browser's clock.
    - Answer: the lb's `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` gains `Date`, so a page Worker reads api's clock from its answers and signs its DPoP `iat` by it. §18.15's list, warp's `addCorsHeaders` and the pagestack fixture's list each gain the one word; no api shape changes and no metadata row is new. An answer with no Date still leaves a skewed sidecar refused, and a page-leg test shows a skewed Worker renewing; the owner applies the lb change with warp's next deploy (§7.4's clock row, §18.15's services row).
    - Cost: one word in three lists.
    - Not taken: (2) a `server_time` field in the three routes' answers, refusals included, which changes their shapes and needs a §2.8 row; (3) the page Worker left on the browser's clock, so a browser more than 60 s off cannot renew or exchange.
    - Raised by the build (the dpop-iat-offset track; the build's ledger, QD-776).

489. **A remote-mode app gets its device's edge-login statement through a Confirm control method, signed only for a confirmed call (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-14).**
    - Question: §13.7's members-device login needs a `SIGNED_TLS_EDGE_LOGIN` by a consented device's identity key, and §18.5's `SignEdgeLogin` signs in process on phones, but a desktop app runs in remote mode, its device the daemon, which offered no control method for the statement, so a desktop app could not complete a members-device login.
    - Answer: the daemon gains a control method, `tls.edge_login.sign`, that signs the device's `TlsEdgeLogin` over an origin and a nonce. TLS owns it, beside `Sidecar.SignTlsEdgeLogin`, and the core's table registers it as a Confirm method, so the daemon signs only for a call confirmed on /dev/tty or in an app's UI, as §5.8's person acts are (D-CORE-13). The sdk's remote backend sends `SignEdgeLogin` through it with `confirmed` set, since the bound call is the person's act in the app's UI, as `AnnounceRepo` and `RevokeRepo` already go. An unconfirmed call is refused and signs nothing, so no same-user process, an agent's included, can vouch as the device for a login the person never saw. The page Worker's path is unchanged (§5.8, §13.7, §18.5).
    - Cost: a control method and a confirmation for each desktop login, or opening the login in the app taken as that confirmation.
    - Not taken: (2) an unconfirmed control method, so any same-user process vouches as the device; (3) no remote path, so a desktop app without the extension cannot complete the login.
    - Raised by the build (SDK-M6-03; the build's ledger, QD-827).

490. **The sdk gains two forwarding exports: `Sidecar.SetForwardingDevice`, which apps call at each start, and a `DeviceLocal` constructor that takes the forwarding key store for C ABI services (option 1, the recommendation, in each, 2026-11-15).**
    - Question: forwarding's setup needs the app's `Device` to reach its VPN component, and the only bound call that gave the `Sidecar` one was `EnrollWithDevice`, so after a restart a setup the core found due (the 90-day rotation, no live Delegation) waited for an enrollment; and the Windows and Linux app services, which bind through the C ABI, cross `DeviceLocalSettings` as json, where `SetForwardKeyStore` has no entry, so their VPN components set up no forwarding, against owner decision 197.
    - Answer: the sdk gains `Sidecar.SetForwardingDevice(device Device)`, which an app calls at each start once it has its `Device` (a `DeviceLocal` in process on Android, a `DeviceRemote` over the device RPC to the Apple extensions): it hands the sidecar role the device without re-pairing, and the role checks what forwarding setup is due then (`ForwardingSetupDue`: the 90-day rotation, none live, a new client generation the core finds) and at every interval after, running the setup as it does after `EnrollWithDevice`, which keeps its meaning; the setup stays the person's act only where §18.6 says, after a foreign revocation. And a bound constructor takes the forwarding key store beside `DeviceLocal`'s settings, `NewDeviceLocalWithForwardKeyStore`, with `NewDeviceLocal`'s parameters and a final `SidecarForwardKeyStore`, exported by gomobile and by the C ABI, where the key store crosses as function pointers as other callback types do; `DeviceLocalSettings.SetForwardKeyStore` stays for gomobile callers, and the json settings stay data. Both join the binding's export tables (§18.5's bound calls and gate table, §18.6's setup, §18.15).
    - Cost: two exports in the binding's tables; the apps call `SetForwardingDevice` at each start.
    - Not taken: for QD-837, (2) `EnrollWithDevice` taking the device without re-pairing on an enrolled sidecar, which changes its re-pair rule, and (3) as built, so a due setup waits for an app run that enrolls; for QD-844, (2) a hand-written C export outside the generator, and (3) C ABI hosts without forwarding until app work needs it.
    - Raised by the build (SDK-M8-04, SDK-M8-05; the build's ledger, QD-837, QD-844).

491. **The Apple network extensions read a base's config head with the replaying head reader every other leg runs, and the forwarding leg's budget rises to 7.5 MiB, amending owner decision 215 (the recommendations, 2026-11-15 and 2026-11-16; Q-SDK-7).**
    - Question: a forwarding role compiles from its newest base, so it reads the config head that base names, and the replaying `EvsConfigHeadReader` owner decisions 317 and 318 gave every leg needs evs files the `ios_extension` leg left out by tag; with the reader on the leg, the leg added 7,009,216 B stripped, past owner decision 215's 6,291,456 B.
    - Answer: the Apple network extensions read a base's config head with the same replaying reader as the other legs: the push record's content and payload readers, the export key and its prefix check move into a leg file, with the export's part read count a settings field (owner decision 297), the seal part record's parser goes on the leg, the reader and the forwarding heads file are untagged for the leg and sdk's extension heads file folds into the common one, so the extensions call the same `ConfigHeads`; the git export machinery stays off the leg, the leg's listing is regenerated in the same commit, and the replay holds the head's files in memory up to the compile cap, as the compile does. The leg's executable budget rises from 6 MiB to 7.5 MiB (7,864,320 B), measured stripped as the shipped binding is: measured on 2026-11-15 with go1.27.4, the leg adds 7,009,216 B with the reader, 1,000,736 B of it the reader and the evs client it links, with no new package, leaving 855,104 B of room. The signed extension's 54 MiB ceiling stays. This is the revisit with measurements that §18.10 and owner decision 251 foresaw; a read-only evs core for the leg's reader is review-phase work beside owner decision 366's storage-backed replay, where the leg's memory is measured on a device (§18.6's follow row, §18.10, D-SDK-20, Q-SDK-7, §4.4, §4.9's size gate, §22.9, §23.6).
    - Cost: about 1 MiB more executable in the extension, and some static memory in a tunnel measured at 19.6 MiB connected against a 28 MiB target.
    - Not taken: for QD-839, (2) a forwarding-only head reader in a bridge file, a second copy of the replay to keep in step, and (3) no head reader in the Apple extensions, so forwarding there never compiles; for QD-973, (2) holding 6 MiB and first cutting the reader's evs client to a read-only core, an EVS-lane refactor of unknown yield with the Apple extensions pending until it lands, and (3) the Apple extensions left without a head reader.
    - Raised by the build (SDK-M8-04 and the ios-leg-head-reader track; the build's ledger, QD-839, QD-973).

492. **The page Worker's loader starts sidecar.wasm with `GODEBUG=gcstoptheworld=1` (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-15).**
    - Question: js/wasm has one thread and no preemption, so Go's background mark worker runs only when the program blocks; a long CPU-bound call can leave a GC cycle open while its allocations are marked live, and linear memory, which never shrinks, grew to 2.8 GB under the Worker harness for tests whose live heaps peaked at 65 MB.
    - Answer: the page Worker's loader starts sidecar.wasm with `go.env = {GODEBUG: "gcstoptheworld=1"}` set before `go.run`, the one variable `make check`'s Worker harness gives its programs; under it the goroutine that triggers a GC yields to a dedicated worker that marks to the end before anything else runs, so a long CPU-bound call (an MLS commit over a large group, decrypting a large repository) cannot leave a cycle open while linear memory grows. The page Worker's boot test holds a planted allocation loop (a 4 MiB heap of pointers, then 256 MiB allocated in 64 KiB blocks) under a bound on linear memory, as the harness's test does, and sdk's own test harness gives its binaries the same environment, so tests show what the shipped Worker does. GOMEMLIMIT and a yield rule are not used (§18.10's wasm leg, D-SDK-19).
    - Cost: one loader line and one test; GC pauses become whole cycles, which on one thread they already are in effect.
    - Not taken: (2) the default, with long loops on the Worker's paths yielding, a rule nothing checks; (3) `GOMEMLIMIT` in the loader, which moves only the next goal and does not end a cycle that cannot finish.
    - Raised by the build (the make-check track; the build's ledger, QD-909).

493. **Desktop behavior selects on gomobile's `macos` tag as well as on GOOS, since the pinned gomobile builds the macOS slices with GOOS=ios, and the leg tables model those slices so (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-16; it completes owner decision 277).**
    - Question: the golang.org/x/mobile sdk pins builds the macOS slices with GOOS=ios plus the `macos` tag (cmd/gomobile/env.go's platformOS, go.dev/issue/52299), so on the macOS app every file a `!ios` or `ios` constraint selects goes as on the iPhone, and its `NewSidecarRemote` answered that remote mode runs on desktops alone; owner decision 277 named the tag but not the GOOS.
    - Answer: desktop behavior selects on `macos` as well as on GOOS: sdk's two remote dial files become `!ios_extension && !js && ((ios && !macos) || android)` and `!ios_extension && !js && (!ios || macos) && !android`, and the root's daemon client files that carry `!ios` gain `|| macos` the same way, the daemon's server files staying out through `!sdk_mobile_bind`; so the macOS app's `NewSidecarRemote` reaches `ur daemon` as §18.4, §18.5 and R-SDK-9 say, and one xcframework is kept. The leg tables model the macOS slices as gomobile builds them, GOOS=ios with `macos` (sdk's messaging boundary legs for macos/arm64 and macos/amd64, and check/'s mirror), and the root's desktop client files are checked to build under GOOS=ios with `macos`. If the daemon client cannot run under GOOS=ios, a separate GOOS=darwin bind for the macOS slices is a new question. Until built, the macOS app's remote mode refuses, the closed direction (§4.7's legs, §5.10, §18.4, §18.5, §18.10).
    - Cost: a cross-lane change of build constraints and leg rows, and a check that the root's desktop client files build under GOOS=ios.
    - Not taken: (2) a separate GOOS=darwin bind for the macOS slices, a second toolchain pin and two xcframeworks; (3) no remote mode on the macOS app, a design change against R-SDK-9.
    - Raised by the build (SDK-M10-01; the build's ledger, QD-991).

## The build's owner questions on server services (2026-11-06 to 2026-11-16)

The design-records track for §19 and §20 folded the answers below on 2026-11-16, each citing its ledger id: the owner-class items the owner accepted in bulk on 2026-11-06, and QD-022, which it decided that day against the recommendation; the answers it decided one at a time from 2026-11-10 to 2026-11-14; and the recommendations of 2026-11-15 and 2026-11-16 that the owner's instruction of 2026-11-16 accepted. A decision that groups several answers carries its latest answer's date. The section's other items, the defect fixes the owner accepted on 2026-11-06 and the build's readings, fills and settled items, went into §19 and the sections they name as text with no number; QD-539's reading, which added `p0_enforce` to §19.11's list, fell to owner decision 510, which retires the key.

494. **The lb keeps reading platform certificates from their versioned paths, and W5's `tls/current` swap stays built but unused (option 4, the owner's choice against the recommendation, 2026-11-06).**
    - Question: W5 has the lb read `tls/current/<name>`, swapped before a reload, but nothing said who owns those links on an lb host, and playbook-edges.yml's copy from vault writes through a host link into a version whose key server/session/by_jwt_keyring.go may verify tokens with.
    - Answer: the lb stays on versioned paths for now. Nothing changes in production: no vault entry or lb image reads `tls/current`, W5's swap stays built but unused, and its operations task builds against a test vault, so new certificates keep arriving with the daily lb deploy. The question returns before D-OPS-12's 45-day lifetimes are due (2028-02-16) (§19.5's W5, §19.11, D-OPS-12).
    - Cost: none now; renewal stays the daily deploy's until the question returns.
    - Not taken: (1) hosts owning the links, a services.yml name list selecting them and `warpctl certs swap` moving them, the recommendation, which costs a new warp key and a swap before a host's first lb start; (2) hosts owning the links with vault entries selecting them, so every future copy path must strip links or a key is overwritten; (3) the vault owning real directories, so a swap is not atomic.
    - Raised by the build (the warp W5 and W6 track; the build's ledger, QD-022).

495. **tlsedge and mxedge's router read Postgres through restricted roles of their own, from `pg-<service>.yml`, holding their maps in memory (option 1, the recommendation, in each, 2026-11-06 and 2026-11-16).**
    - Question: §13.5's cached label map had no source, §19.4 listed no Postgres credential for the edges while §13.9, tls-3, §15's tables and §19.7's pools assumed one, and §19.10 said the edges run from Redis; and once restricted roles were chosen, warp mounts a vault file under its own name while server's pool read only `pg.yml`.
    - Answer: tlsedge reads Postgres, as §13.9 and tls-3 have it, through a restricted role in its own vault file: SELECT on `dns_label`, `network_dns_custom_host`, `network_tls_suspension` and §12.9's suspensions, INSERT on `tls_connection_record`, nothing else. `TlsEdgeStore` is a background loop: a full snapshot at start, then `model.DnsLabelMapSince` every 5 s, so a withdrawn name stops mapping within 10 s; kills still arrive within 5 s through `{tes_}` and reload every 60 s; records go in 10 s batches off the path. In a Postgres outage a running process keeps its last snapshot and one started then stays not ready, so the edges run from memory and Redis and a restart waits for Postgres; no connection path touches Postgres (I-12). mxedge's router takes a scoped role the same way, used only from batch goroutines, and the platform role needs no `MxEdgeStore`, its readiness waiving it. server's pool reads `pg-<service>.yml` when a block mounts one and falls back to `pg.yml` otherwise, as `grafana-push-<service>.yml` is read first, so tlsedge reads `pg-tlsedge.yml` and the router `pg-mxedge.yml`, never the platform's `pg.yml` (D-OPS-1); the owner creates each role and its file, and until then each block's store cannot connect and the block stays not ready (§19.4, §19.7, §19.10, §19.17; §13.5; §2.8's tls-1).
    - Cost: a scoped credential in a hostile-facing process on every lb block, as mxedge's and smtpedge's tables already implied; a restart during a Postgres outage waits for Postgres; one resource lookup in server's pool.
    - Not taken: for QD-029, (2) an internal api surface, another credential and api in the path of a restart, and (3) a Redis projection, a third copy of the label table with a rebuild path; for QD-992, (2) a per-service path in warp's scoped mount, a warp change on every host, and (3) the platform's `pg.yml` on both blocks, dropping the restriction.
    - Raised by the build (the TLS and MAIL plans, OPS-M5-03; the build's ledger, QD-029, QD-992).

496. **The minio fork exports each set's partial-write queue, its drops and unclean starts and each cluster's last full heal, by which a set leaves `degraded`, and a minio task and an xops task build the two signal sources (the recommendations, 2026-11-06 and 2026-11-12).**
    - Question: MinIO's admin API shows no partial-write queue, the queue drops new entries silently when full and a crash loses it, and the scanner reaches an object about once in 1,024 cycles, so §19.6's "heal pending (MRF or scanner)" could not be read and a set could show `protected` over a missing shard; and then no task built the fork's export or the scrape of each cluster's node metrics that monitor reads.
    - Answer: the fork's `er.addPartial` sets SetIndex and PoolIndex, and cmd/mrf.go exports per pool and set the queue's depth, a count of dropped entries and a count of unclean starts, with each cluster's last full heal time; monitor exports them as `urnetwork_filecluster_mrf_depth{cluster,set}`, `urnetwork_filecluster_mrf_dropped_total{cluster,set}` and `urnetwork_filecluster_unclean_starts_total{cluster,set}`. A set is `degraded` with a drive or host offline or a depth above 0; it leaves when a full heal ends with every drive online, or, if no drive or host was offline, when its depth reaches 0 with no drop or unclean start since the last full heal; a drop or unclean start with every drive online warns and starts one full heal. A minio task builds the fork's half; an xops task grows the file clusters' playbook with a Fluent Bit scrape of each cluster's MinIO node metrics into Mimir (`job file_cluster_minio`, a `cluster` label, a bearer from vault); and monitor's state probe reads the queue. Until both land, heal debt reads unknown, never zero (§19.6, §19.8, §19.10; FS-40; D-OPS-14, D-OPS-20).
    - Cost: one more small patch to carry at each upgrade; a full heal after a rare drop spends HDD operations and NVMe writes the budget counts; two small tasks.
    - Not taken: for QD-035, (1) no patch, `protected` meaning only every drive online and the debt invisible, and (3) no patch with a monthly full heal, a recurring whole-bucket cost; for QD-753, (2) monitor reading each MinIO host's metrics itself, a second request path to every storage host, and (3) dropping the queue rule, so a set never leaves `degraded` without a person saying the heal ended.
    - Raised by the G0 re-checks and the build (S11, EVSB-M3-01; the build's ledger, QD-035, QD-753).

497. **sockets authenticates Client-class callers with no Postgres, running through about an hour of Postgres loss, and reads `revocation_record_enabled` from `config/<env>/sidecar.yml` (option 1, the recommendation, in each, 2026-11-06 and 2026-11-16).**
    - Question: sockets has no Postgres access and no by_jwt keys, yet must verify a Client-class caller and learn its §8 subject; and once the revocation record carried that check, sockets had to read auth.yml's `revocation_record_enabled` without mounting auth.yml, which holds the issuer's signing keys.
    - Answer: Client class stays. sockets mounts a public-key export of the by_jwt keyring in `secret_files`, and session gains a verify-only keyring that rereads it as `byJwtKeyring` does; state comes from the revocation record alone, never SQL: a valid record passes, a tombstone or rotation answers 401, a Redis failure answers `unavailable` and a miss `unavailable` with `retry_after_ms`; a miss within 120 s of the last valid check (`{sc_<client_id>}ok`) counts as valid without moving it, and a revocation in that span shows as a tombstone, which lives 10 min. A token with no root claim is refused, and one without the `sidecar_id` claim, which `POST /sidecar/bind`'s re-mint adds and refresh keeps, is denied unless its network is `urnetwork-edge`. Since a client's projected fields lapse in 54-60 min and only a later check re-projects them, sockets runs through about an hour of Postgres loss and then answers `unavailable`. `revocation_record_enabled` moves from vault's auth.yml into `config/<env>/sidecar.yml`, beside the owner's other switches, a rollout switch and not a secret; session's loader reads it there, so api and sockets read one value, and sockets mounts no auth.yml (D-OPS-1). The rollout moves the switch to config before it is turned on, before M5; clearing it still returns readers to SQL, and until it is built the flag reads false in sockets and every Client-class call answers `unavailable`. Owner decision 347 built the verifier (§19.4, §19.10, §19.11, §19.17; §9.8; §23.4's M5 row).
    - Cost: session gains a verify-only keyring and a no-SQL check; sockets answers `unavailable` past about an hour of Postgres loss; one switch moves from vault to config, with a rollout step.
    - Not taken: for QD-040, (2) a sockets service-token audience, whose exchange needs Postgres every 15 min, (3) the signed binding in every call, which still needs option 1's keyring, and (4) signing keys and a Postgres handle on sockets; for QD-993, (2) a vault file holding only the flag, a second copy that can disagree, and (3) auth.yml on sockets, putting the issuer's keys on a hostile-facing block.
    - Raised by the build (the SOCK plan, OPS-M5-03; the build's ledger, QD-040, QD-993).

498. **A commercial pager service the owner picks owns rotas, acknowledgment and the 30-minute escalation, and business-hours pages wait for the business calendar (option 1, the recommendation, in each, 2026-11-06 and 2026-11-12).**
    - Question: §19.8's rota needed a pager, and nothing said whether a vendor or monitor owns the escalation; and the pager pages at any hour, while §19.8's business rows page only in business hours.
    - Answer: a commercial pager service, reached from outside the site, owns rotas, acknowledgment and the 30-minute escalation; the owner picks the vendor. monitor posts events with a dedup key through `pager.Sink`, a generic HTTPS event client, and page bodies hold class, time and opaque ids only, never a network name, address or token; a fake sink models the policy for FS-50 at T3, and the real policy is proved on the out-of-hours drill; §2.8's ext-13 lists the vendor before it goes live (I-18). Page classes carry their hours, any or business: the notifier holds a business-hours page until the business calendar opens (model's AbuseCalendar, abuse.yml's review hours, or the pager service's schedule once one is chosen), then pages, resolving it as usual with the pager's dedup and retry unchanged, and drops a page whose alert resolves first. §19.8's business rows page so: a metadata drive's 7-day DWPD, a drive's rating falling short, a cluster's projected exhaustion within its move tool's lead time or at its cohort limit, a segment cluster's projected HDD crossing, and a running NS failing SOA reads for 1 h (§19.8, D-OPS-9; §2.8's ext-13; §23.7).
    - Cost: a vendor account and a new receiver of operational metadata; FS-50's escalation clause is proved only on the real drill; an hours field and a hold in the notifier.
    - Not taken: for QD-234, (2) monitor owning escalation over SMS and phone, making our own process what must not fail during the incident it reports, and (3) Grafana's contact points, which stop with the site; for QD-754, (2) business rows left on Slack, a warn an unread channel loses, and (3) low-urgency pages held by a vendor's schedule, waiting on a vendor not yet chosen.
    - Raised by the build (the OPS plan, EVSB-M3-01; the build's ledger, QD-234, QD-754).

499. **No canary environment is built for: only its cheap parts exist, no canary deploy counts as evidence, and the tier leaves the design unless the owner buys hosts for it (option 3 now, then option 2, the recommendation, 2026-11-06).**
    - Question: §19.11, §19.7, §22.4 and §23 named a canary environment of evs, gkm and sockets with stores rebuilt per merge, but config/canary/ was deleted in 2023, no build leg or host serves it, and nothing rebuilds its stores.
    - Answer: the cheap parts are built now: the `sidecar_pending` tag leg, a `config/canary/` a host could use, and canary kept in `sidecarPendingAllowed`. No plan counts a canary deploy as evidence, since T3 and M2's staging cluster prove stores and hosts and main stays dark behind its gates; the tier leaves §19.7, §19.11, §22.4 and §23 unless the owner buys hosts for it (§19.7, §19.11; §22.4; D-PHASE-7 and §23.15).
    - Cost: a tier on paper; evs, gkm and sockets meet real lb hosts first on main, dark.
    - Not taken: (1) standing canary up, hosts the hardware list does not include and a tier to run through M10; (2) dropping it at once, which the recommendation keeps as the end state.
    - Raised by the build (the OPS plan; the build's ledger, QD-236).

500. **evs reads its block count from sidecar.yml, as tlsedge reads its own, to divide `evs_unpaid_read_rate` (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-10).**
    - Question: `evs_unpaid_read_rate` is a network's rate across every evs block, paced per process, so each process must know how many blocks serve.
    - Answer: an evs block count in config, set by the operator with services.yml's evs block count: `config/<env>/sidecar.yml`'s evs gate carries it as `blocks`, which server/evshost passes as `QuotaGateSettings.Read.Blocks`, with no default, a block whose gate is enabled with no count refusing to start. The read path keeps no shared state; a non-positive limit reads as unpaced, and `FallbackRate` (1 MiB/s split by blocks) paces until limits are read (§19.4's evs row, §19.11; §20.6).
    - Cost: one more number an operator keeps in step with services.yml; a stale count over- or under-paces unpaid reads by that ratio.
    - Not taken: (2) the live count of evs processes from the write budget's reports, which moves during a deploy and puts the read path on Redis soft state; (3) a per-process rate, which changes §20.4's published meaning.
    - Raised by the build (QUOTA-M2-10; the build's ledger, QD-639).

501. **A failed DELETE holds its key until every drive commit of it has answered and each that landed has been reverted, with no revert after release (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-12).**
    - Question: TLC found two DELETEs on one key breaking FI-30 under §19.6's rule (3) as written: a failed DELETE released once fewer than N/2 drives could hold it could still land or revert a late commit after another request saw the key.
    - Answer: §19.6's rule (3) holds a failed DELETE's key until every one of its drive commits has answered and each that landed has been reverted, with no revert after release; a failed PUT keeps rule (3) as written, released once the drives that may still hold it number under N/2. Nothing of a failed DELETE then lands or reverts once another request can see the key, so FI-30's NoStaleQuorum holds for two DELETEs on one key, which §11.7's marker collection, v1's purge and the drop job rely on. The cost, a DELETE whose commit stalled on a frozen host holding its key until that host answers or its drives are replaced, is paid only by background jobs' unconditional DELETEs, as D-OPS-20's Why and FI-30's last clause say; the in-house build already holds a failed DELETE so, and StoreSettle.tla models the held DELETE and passes TLC with DELETEs in the run at M2's Gn-in (§19.6, D-OPS-20, §19.17; FI-30).
    - Cost: a DELETE stalled on a frozen host holds its key until the host answers or its drives are replaced, a longer hold than D-OPS-20's Why priced, paid by background jobs' DELETEs only.
    - Not taken: (2) releasing below N/2 with no revert after release and a drive counting a missing key only if no earlier commit on it is unsettled, which leaves the first trace until heal runs; (3) keeping the rule and stating that a GET may answer stale meanwhile, so FI-30 fails as written.
    - Raised by the build's models (EVSB-M2-22, StoreSettle.tla; the build's ledger, QD-744).

502. **`sidecarctl certs renew` and `warpctl certs issue` stay on lego v4.35.2, the release production runs, with lego v5 recorded as D-OPS-12's target (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-12).**
    - Question: D-OPS-12 names lego v5, but v5 rejects the flags both commands pass, and no one in the run may fetch the image to read v5's CLI.
    - Answer: both commands stay on goacme/lego v4.35.2 with certs issue's exact flags plus `--server`, held together by TestCertsRenewLegoArgsAreTheImagesCli and TestCertsRenewRunsTheLegoReleaseCertsIssueRuns; D-OPS-12's v5 is the target. The move to v5 is one change to both commands and both tests, made once someone allowed to fetch the image has read v5's `--help` or run one order against Let's Encrypt staging, an outward action outside the run. Meanwhile renewals send no RFC 9773 `replaces`, which costs the CA's rate-limit exemption for a renewal it asked for and does not change when a certificate renews; sidecarctl reads ARI itself (D-OPS-12).
    - Cost: renewals send no `replaces`; v4 eventually stops getting fixes.
    - Not taken: (2) both commands moved to v5 from its documentation, an argument list no machine here has run, on an unattended job holding vault-write credentials; (3) an ACME library in sidecarctl, with DNS-01 solvers and more modules on its leg.
    - Raised by the build (OPS-M5-07; the build's ledger, QD-751).

503. **The competition's migration for owner decision 267 lands in two steps, the permissive one in MG0's pending part (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-12).**
    - Question: owner decision 267 adds sidecar to the competition's source graphs, which server's database checks, and that migration had no place on server's `sidecar` branch.
    - Answer: the permissive step, replacing `competition_v2_source_graph_valid` and `competition_v2_source_authorized` so a source graph of ten keys, or of eleven with sidecar, passes, goes in MG0's pending part (db_migrations_sidecar_pending.go) and registers with MG0 at M0's Gn-out, with server's Go list of eleven, its digest and the evaluator scripts. The tightening step, requiring eleven, is a cleanup migration once every competition job runs a release that sends eleven, in connect/CODESTYLE.md's additive-then-cleanup order. TestSidecarGroupsAreAdditive admits a function replacement that only widens a guard. Evaluator images are rebuilt from the Gn-out's commit; that rebuild and the cleanup's deploy are outward steps outside the run (§19.7's MG0 row).
    - Cost: §19.7's MG0 row gains a competition entry, and the additive test admits a widening function replacement.
    - Not taken: (2) a pending group of its own, a kind §19.7 does not name; (3) the change landing on `main` before M0's Gn-out, outside this run.
    - Raised by the build (the wire-consumers track; the build's ledger, QD-752).

504. **A file cluster in settle mode has at least 4 hosts: the in-house build refuses to start a one-process cluster so, and T3's second metadata cluster becomes 4 × 2 (option 2 with option 1's crash fixed, the recommendation, 2026-11-12).**
    - Question: on a one-process cluster the minio fork's settle build took a DELETE past its deadline and crashed in its stash sweep on a nil lock server.
    - Answer: a one-process MinIO cluster is out of scope for production, since §19.6 builds every cluster from 4 hosts (owner decision 145's whole clusters): the build refuses to start one in settle mode, naming the rule, and whatever the shape its local locker's settle rows and stash sweep never dereference a nil locker. T3's second metadata cluster becomes a 4 × 2 cluster like the first, so FS-63's second-cluster cases and the file-cluster suite run on a multi-host cluster, and the suite's one-process path goes (§19.6, D-OPS-20; §22.4).
    - Cost: four more containers in T3.
    - Not taken: (1) alone, a one-process cluster keeping every settle rule, a fork change for a shape production never runs; (3) leaving the crash, and a deadline rule silently off, on any one-process deployment.
    - Raised by the build (EVSB-M2-19; the build's ledger, QD-756).

505. **api answers a request whose Postgres connection a restart cut with 503 and `Retry-After: 1`, as it answers a lock timeout (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-12).**
    - Question: §22.8's Postgres or PgBouncer restart row expects retryable answers, but api answered a cut connection with its fixed 500, which means a failure that will repeat.
    - Answer: RaiseHttpError (server/router/handler_utils.go) and the router's recover map the connection classes 08000, 08003, 08006, 57P01, 57P02 and 57P03 to 503 with `Retry-After: 1`, the cause staying in the log, so a client tells a restart from a failure that will repeat and §22.8's row holds its retryable clause as written. A route whose effect may have committed before the cut keeps relying on its own idempotency (admin-token claims, sidecar push ids). Other internal failures keep the fixed 500 (§19.10).
    - Cost: a few lines in server/router and a test.
    - Not taken: (2) keeping the 500 and reading the row as "a retry is safe", so a 500 means two things and alerting counts every restart.
    - Raised by the build (TEST-M2-15; the build's ledger, QD-759).

506. **After a restore, an orphaned authorization log's repos stay active for 7 days and are then shredded under the incident (option 3, the recommendation, 2026-11-14).**
    - Question: §19.10 marked each repo of a log whose network the restored rows lack `deleted{deletion_id = the incident id}`, but repo/'s deleted state names the RepoDelete's signer, and an incident signed nothing.
    - Answer: an orphaned `proj/` log's repos are not marked deleted: repo/ stays active, and nothing reaches it meanwhile, since the lost network's tokens are refused. The `sidecar_restore_orphan_log` job waits 7 d 10 min from the incident, re-checks that the network's rows are still absent and cancels if they came back, moves each repo active → shredding{incident} with If-Match, which needs no signer, as a DELETE with `now` does, queues §11.12's shred for each, and deletes the log's `proj/` and `projrows/` after the last repo is shredded. No format or D- item changes, and CheckEvsRepoTransition's signer rule (owner decision 343) stands (§19.10's postgres step; FS-42).
    - Cost: repo/ shows active, not deleted, for the 7 days.
    - Not taken: (1) the incident id as `state_signer`, a field meaning the RepoDelete's signer holding an id that signed nothing; (2) a deleted state naming no signer for a restore's incident, a format rule change.
    - Raised by the build (OPS-M3-03; the build's ledger, QD-813).

507. **evs counts what observe kept and labels the versionless sweep's DELETEs by pass, and monitor warns when purge has been on for a day with no turn-on pass (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-15).**
    - Question: monitor was to warn when observe's retained count stays flat for a day while purge is on, but no series counted what observe kept.
    - Answer: evsservice labels the versionless sweep's DELETEs by pass, `urnetwork_evs_versionless_sweep_deletes_total{pass="evacuation"|"hold_end"|"turn_on",result}`, and the Purger and the sweep count what observe kept, `urnetwork_evs_versionless_observed_total`; §19.8's warn fires when purge has been on in the env's file for a day, the observed count rose at some time before, and no `pass="turn_on"` DELETE happened in the last day, the sign that `sidecarctl evs purge-turn-on` was not run. No state is shared across processes, and monitor reads only counters (§19.8).
    - Cost: a label and a counter in evsservice, and one more partition in monitor's query.
    - Not taken: (2) the D-TEST-12 checker exporting a gauge the turn-on pass zeroes, state shared across taskworker processes; (3) dropping the warn, so a forgotten command leaves observe's content until someone notices the storage.
    - Raised by the build (EVS2-M10-34; the build's ledger, QD-855).

508. **`{sso_}`, `{oauthdev_}` and `{oauthuser_}` join §19.7's Redis tags and the rdb bundles' filter (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-15).**
    - Question: the IdP sign-in flow state `{sso_<state>}` (10 min, taken once) holds a nonce, a PKCE verifier and who began a sign-in, and was in neither §19.7's tags nor D-OPS-16's filter, so a flow in flight at a dump would sit in ops-3's rdb bundles for about 8 months.
    - Answer: `{sso_}` joins §19.7's Redis tags (D-OPS-7; it expires well within 48 h), and `'{sso_'` joins xops's bringyour-redis-filter.sh `sidecar_key_prefixes` with its SIDECAR_KEYS test key in the same change, so a flow in flight at a dump never sits in ops-3's bundles and api-3's published ttl holds (D-TRUST-7), as owner decision 409 did for `{evsc_}`. The device authorization families `{oauthdev_}` and `{oauthuser_}` (10 min, taken once, holding a device code's hash, a user code and a DPoP thumbprint) take the same rule in the same change. Until it lands, team IdP sign-in and the device grant stay off where rdb bundles are taken (§19.7, D-OPS-16).
    - Cost: one OPS line and its test keys, no server code.
    - Not taken: (2) leaving the families in the bundles and rewording api-3 to an 8-month ttl for a record holding one-time secrets.
    - Raised by the build (the SSO flow store; the build's ledger, QD-889).

509. **xops's file-cluster playbook owns the storage hosts' Fluent Bit, and their 5 s drive counters run in a second Fluent Bit process of their own (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-15).**
    - Question: the 5 s drive counters and the MinIO node scrape need Fluent Bit on the storage hosts, which no playbook installed, and one Fluent Bit process runs only one node_exporter_metrics input.
    - Answer: playbook-file-clusters.yml owns the storage hosts' Fluent Bit as playbook-minio-clusters.yml owns edge-5's: the shared install block, the shared config as base.conf, and a host template composing it with the MinIO scrape (owner decision 496). The 5 s counters run in a second Fluent Bit process of their own unit, whose one node_exporter_metrics input (diskstats and filesystem) serves port 2021 on the storage LAN, reachable only from taskworker hosts, so each process keeps one such input; the SMART and clock textfiles link into the shared input's directory, and remote-write to Mimir stays for monitor (§19.6's counters row).
    - Cost: one more unit on four hosts.
    - Not taken: (2) one process scraping every 5 s with an exporter output, so every node series of those hosts is remote-written three times as often; (3) the storage hosts in an edge host group, whose playbooks carry warp, docker and edge policy a storage host should not get.
    - Raised by the build (the file-cluster signal sources; the build's ledger, QD-890).

510. **sidecar.yml's `p0_enforce` key is retired: §7.4's stage 1 and D-TEAM-14 refuse in code from M1, whatever the file says (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-15).**
    - Question: a reading kept `p0_enforce: {enabled: false}` in sidecar.yml as M0's log-only switch and added it to §19.11's list, but since M1 the route gates, session's strict_exp expiry and the transport lease's end refuse in code, so the key reads like a kill switch and is not one.
    - Answer: the key is retired. §7.4's stage 1 and D-TEAM-14 refuse in code (api/route_authz.go's route gates, session's strict_exp expiry, the transport lease's end) whatever sidecar.yml says or whether it is there, so enforcement never depends on a config file. §19.11's list does not carry the key; server's `SidecarConfig` loses `P0Enforce` and its yaml field, a file that still holds the line reading as one without it, and its test takes another gate as the observable of a fail-closed read; config files drop the line. M0's Gn-out is unaffected: M0's pinned binary reads an absent key as false, log-only (§19.11; §7.4).
    - Cost: one small operations change.
    - Not taken: (2) the key kept parsed and inert, a misleading key in every environment's file.
    - Raised by the build (TEAM-M1-01; the build's ledger, QD-922, which drops QD-539's reading).

511. **The minio fork answers a read 404 only when a read quorum of the object's drives report it absent, and 503 when the drives it reached make no read quorum either way (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-15).**
    - Question: with drives offline the fork answered 404 for an acknowledged object it could not reach a read quorum of, and evs acts on a 404 as definite: an absent head is a new ref, GC and evacuation read an absent object as gone, and an outcome read after an unknown PUT sends the bytes again.
    - Answer: the urnetwork/minio fork answers an object read 404 only when a read quorum of the object's drives positively report it absent. A drive that is offline, unreachable or not yet reconnected counts as unknown, never absent, so when the drives reached make a read quorum neither for the object nor for its absence the fork answers 503 (InsufficientReadQuorum): an acknowledged object that 3 drives hold, 1 lacks and 4 cannot be reached reads 503, as does a read through a host whose data path has not reconnected after a restart. This is what §19.6 and D-OPS-20 promise, a read seeing the acknowledged state or failing, and what FI-30, FS-1's outcome read and FS-40's "unreadable" assume; evs is unchanged and never consults drive states. §22.9's file-cluster suite gains the case: a DriveMiss'd object with two hosts down reads 503, and while a host reconnects every host serves it or answers 503, never 404 (§19.6's read row, D-OPS-20; FS-40, §22.9).
    - Cost: a change in the fork's read path and its tests.
    - Not taken: (2) evs treating a 404 from a cluster with a drive offline as unknown, which every reader of every family would need drive states for; (3) accepting it, with FS-40 asserting any failure, which hides the 404 evs acts on.
    - Raised by the build (TEST-M2-07; the build's ledger, QD-962).

512. **sidecarctl gains `evs drop-reindex`, which rebuilds a drop job's corrupt list files through `DropResolve.Reindex` (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-15).**
    - Question: a drop job's list file that fails its trailer pages as `drop_index_corrupt` and is to be rewritten by an operations command, and none existed.
    - Answer: sidecarctl (server/sidecarctl, owner decision 274) gains `evs drop-reindex <repo_id> <job_id> [<drop_id>:<ref_id>:<version>:<push_id>...]`. It resolves the repo's metadata and segment clusters through `place/` and calls `(*evsservice.DropResolve).Reindex` with a host whose Blobs reach both: with no drops named, Reindex takes them from the first of the job's list files that reads whole; it recomputes the job's list files as resolve does, deletes each that fails its trailer or layout or holds other bytes by a DELETE naming its LastModified, refused while the segment cluster has not passed the settle cases, and writes it again; a drop that no longer resolves is an error, and nothing is written. It prints the sequences rewritten, written fresh and found stray, ids only, never a path (I-13), and exits non-zero on any error; the file clusters' runbook row for the page names it (§19.8).
    - Cost: one small operations task.
    - Not taken: (2) a taskworker one-off, a runbook step with no tool behind it.
    - Raised by the build (EVSB-M6-01; the build's ledger, QD-965).

513. **sidecarctl gains `evs observe-report <env>`, which prints the purge and drop soaks, and each drop job records whether its listed versions renamed a path or copied across refs (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-16).**
    - Question: D-TEST-12's purge soak and D-TEST-18's drop soak gate turning purge and drop on, and nothing printed them; D-TEST-18 also asks that half its jobs be on repos with renames and cross-ref copies, which nothing recorded.
    - Answer: sidecarctl gains `evs observe-report <env>`, beside `evs purge-turn-on` in server/sidecarctl (owner decision 274). It reads the counter `urnetwork_evs_versionless_observe_total` (results clean and retained) as daily samples over the soak from monitor's query API, as §19.8's rows read counters, and the drop tables (`drops/<repo>/_`) of the env's format-2 repos through `place/`, and prints whether each soak is met, exiting 0 either way: it only reads, so `purge: on` and `drop: on` stay an operator's act. Each drop job records two booleans in its first snapshot file: renames (a listed version's push removed one path and created another citing its kind-5 content) and cross-ref copies (a listed version's chain named a reuse source of another ref); a job counts toward D-TEST-18's share when both hold, and §2.8's evs-28 names them (§19.11; §2.8's evs-28).
    - Cost: one operations task, a small change to the drop job's snapshot file and a row's words.
    - Not taken: (2) the command with no classification, so D-TEST-18's share has no evidence; (3) no command, the counter and drop statuses read by hand.
    - Raised by the build (EVS2-M10-33; the build's ledger, QD-987).

## The build's owner questions on tiers, quotas, metering and billing (2026-11-06 to 2026-11-16)

The design-records track for §19 and §20 folded the answers below on 2026-11-16, each citing its ledger id: the owner-class items the owner accepted in bulk on 2026-11-06, the answers it decided one at a time on 2026-11-10, and the recommendations of 2026-11-15 that the owner's instruction of 2026-11-16 accepted. A decision that groups several answers carries its latest answer's date. The section's other items, the defect fixes the owner accepted on 2026-11-06 and the build's readings and fills, went into §20 and the sections they name as text with no number; the fill QD-510 (closure pacing's 5-minute horizon) fell to owner decision 516, and the reading QD-295 (the downgrade clock from a renewal's end) to owner decision 523.

514. **`RefreshTeamTransferBalances` writes each Team period's grant and credit line at its anchor, and `invoice.paid` writes only the first (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-06).**
    - Question: a Team's cycle invoice is finalized 72 h into its period at the earliest (§20.9's grace), so `invoice.paid`, which wrote each period's `team` grant and credit line, arrived after the period it paid for had begun.
    - Answer: `RefreshTeamTransferBalances` writes each period's `team` grant and `team_credit` line at its 00:00:00 UTC anchor for every Team whose subscription is live with no unresolved payment failure. `invoice.paid` writes only the first period; on a cycle invoice it records the renewal's revenue, clears dunning and restores a line a failure ended. `invoice.payment_failed` stops the next grant and ends the current line, so overage refuses at once while included bytes run out. Exposure per unpaid Team is one period: its base, its seats and up to 10 TiB of line paid out at $3 per TiB (§20.5, §20.9, D-QUOTA-8).
    - Cost: a period's balances are granted before its invoice is collected, a risk owner decision 92 already accepts for overage, now also for the base.
    - Not taken: (2) `invoice.paid` the only writer, with Team windows given about 5 days of grace, so a lapsed Team keeps its tier 5 days and overlapping windows double the included bytes; (3) base and overage in two subscriptions, against §20.9's one subscription per Team network.
    - Raised by the build (the QUOTA plan; the build's ledger, QD-020).

515. **A Team member is active, and its seat billed, while any client under its `member_id` has an `auth_time` within 30 days (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-06).**
    - Question: §20.5 bills seats for members "active within 30 days", but `network_member` has no such column and §2.8 lists none, and R-QUOTA-6 promises active people only.
    - Answer: a member is active while any client under its `member_id` has an `auth_time` within 30 days, the existing column, as the billing terms state, so an always-on device or a member's agent keeps that seat billed; Auditor and Billing admin never count (§20.5's seats).
    - Cost: unattended devices and a member's agents keep a seat billed.
    - Not taken: (2) a person's own sign-in or approval only, closer to "active people" but a new column and a §2.8 row.
    - Raised by the build (the QUOTA plan; the build's ledger, QD-143).

516. **Closure pacing admits by seat, each seat good only in its own second, and a refused ask records nothing under its debit id (option 4, the recommendation, 2026-11-10).**
    - Question: §20.7's pacing, built as a bucket with a slot tail, admitted by arrival, so four erasers could take every turn, and an eraser arriving late at each second's start took the capacity of the erasers whose slots lay in that second.
    - Answer: closure pacing admits by seat. A seat is a 1/R cell at the tail of the network's pacing, good from its instant to the end of its calendar second; a seat is handed out only in a second with a free cell; an eraser whose seat is due takes, and nothing else is checked, so arrival order decides nothing. A refusal is not recorded under its debit id, so the same upload asks again. An eraser that misses its seat's second by more than the cell's tolerance (250 ms to 1 s at R = 4) is seated again, at once while the second has a free cell and one round later in a full network. The fill's 5-minute horizon and its setting go, and the steady state is the landed one, k erasers each every k/R s (§20.7's closure and wait rows, §20.14's `{sq_}` row; DESIGN-DETAILS.md section 13).
    - Cost: the pacing, its fake, the `{sq_}` script and quotasuite's pacing cases change together.
    - Not taken: (1) the bucket as built, fairness resting on the order asks reach Redis; (2) a refused eraser taking a new slot at the tail, which the model shows unbounded; (3) refusals' `retry_after_ms` staggered by rank, still admitting by arrival.
    - Raised by the design details (DESIGN-DETAILS.md section 13; the build's ledger, QD-652, which drops the fill QD-510).

517. **No lease byte is debited without its carve, and a lease's own tokens live 15 minutes, extended while it lives (option 2, the recommendation, 2026-11-10).**
    - Question: a lease's bytes are carved each window into a debit's token; bytes spent under a reservation that has since lapsed had no carve, and a dead edge's 24 h tokens held its network's draws for a day.
    - Answer: no lease byte is debited without its carve: a batch is posted only once its carves answered; bytes whose reservation was gone are dropped from the event, counted in `urnetwork_quota_lease_unbilled_bytes_total` and alerted; `ApplyUsageBatch` refuses a lease meter's event its carves do not cover. A lease's own tokens live 15 min and are extended every 5 min while the lease lives, and the host stops spending a token's bytes 30 s before it could lapse; carved tokens keep 24 h. The cost is under-billing of at most one window's bytes a host during a Redis outage as seen from that host; a dead edge's hold returns in 15 min; `Lease.tla` holds NoOverdraw only with this (§20.6's leases and loss rows, §20.9's E-d; FS-46; §2.8's api-13).
    - Cost: under-billing of at most one window's bytes a host during a Redis outage; one extend call a live draw every 5 min.
    - Not taken: (1) billing them at apply with 24 h tokens, so a near-empty balance can be overdrawn by a host's uncarved window and a dead edge's draws are held a day; (3) option 2 with 24 h lease tokens.
    - Raised by the design details (DESIGN-DETAILS.md section 13; the build's ledger, QD-653).

518. **A lease draws 64 MiB, or 4 s of its own measured rate when that is more, up to 16 times it (option 2, the recommendation, 2026-11-10).**
    - Question: a fixed 64 MiB draw renews twice a second on a fast lease, and a renewal slower than 134 ms resets the streams of a network that has balance.
    - Answer: a lease's draw is 64 MiB (16 MiB on Community) or, when that is more, 4 s of the lease's own measured rate rounded up to a multiple of it, at most 16 times it (1 GiB; 256 MiB on Community), renewed with a quarter of the draw or 2 s of traffic left, whichever is more. A slow lease draws as before; a fast one renews about once per 4 s with 2 s to do it. A lease can hold up to 4 s of its traffic unspent, which a Redis loss can over-admit for it (§20.6's draw row; §2.8's api-13, whose sizes landed with QD-657).
    - Cost: a lease holds up to 4 s of its traffic unspent, which Redis loss can over-admit.
    - Not taken: (1) 64 MiB always; (3) 64 MiB tokens issued ahead by time, sixteen at most, the same hold in sixteen scripts.
    - Raised by the design details (DESIGN-DETAILS.md section 13; the build's ledger, QD-654).

519. **The read-receipt budget's keys are `evs_read_receipts_per_repo_day: 256` and `evs_read_receipt_bytes: 4kib` (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-15).**
    - Question: one fill named the budget `evs_read_receipts_per_repo_day` with the counter key evs uses, and another, listing §20.4's keys, named it `evs_read_receipts_per_day`.
    - Answer: §20.4's `sidecar_abuse:` names the read-receipt budget `evs_read_receipts_per_repo_day: 256` and `evs_read_receipt_bytes: 4kib`, which server/model/pro_sidecar.go and config/all/pro.yml hold; `evs_read_receipts_per_repo_day` is the `limit_key` a QuotaRefusal carries for it, and the rate key maps to it. `evs_read_receipts_per_day` is not used: each area names its keys, and evs is the area here. evs counts with `Quota.CountRate` on the repo's network, the push id its debit id, so a retry counts nothing twice, and past it answers 429 with `retry_after_ms` to the next 00:00 UTC (§20.4; §11.12).
    - Cost: none in code; the plan's Build line and one fill's text change.
    - Not taken: (2) `evs_read_receipts_per_day`, a rename in pro_sidecar.go and pro.yml, a name that hides that the cap is per repo.
    - Raised by the build (QUOTA-M1-01; the build's ledger, QD-917).

520. **`QuotaLimits` carries `enforced`, from `enforce_sidecar_limits`, and a daemon applying limits locally refuses only while it is true (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-15).**
    - Question: refusals ship dark behind `enforce_sidecar_limits`, but `GET /sidecar/limits` served only the plan's tier and limits, so a daemon applying them locally (the team directory's request limits, the cleanup row's admissions) could not tell a dark limit from an enforced one and would refuse what the server does not.
    - Answer: `QuotaLimits`, Quota.Limits's result and `GET /sidecar/limits`'s body, gains `enforced bool` (JSON `enforced`), which api sets from `enforce_sidecar_limits`. The body keeps reporting the plan's configured `tier` and `limits` whatever the switch says, so `ur status` and an app can show a network its limits before enforcement starts. A daemon or reader that applies limits locally (§16.11's `directory_*` keys at requesting daemons and directory readers, D-QUOTA-11; §7.10's cleanup row's `config_admissions`; any other local pre-check) refuses on a limit only when `enforced` is true; while it is false its local refusals are dark as the server's are (R-QUOTA-5, D-QUOTA-2). The closure floor and pacing are read and applied whatever the flag, since they hold while a network is spent and spent is never dark. An absent field reads false, so an older api's answer refuses nothing locally (§20.5's switch, §20.7, §20.14's routes, D-QUOTA-2; §16.11; §7.10).
    - Cost: one field in an every-leg type before its format freezes.
    - Not taken: (2) the route answering none for each dark key, so the body stops reporting the plan; (3) daemons always applying the limits they read, departing from R-QUOTA-5 for client-applied limits.
    - Raised by the build (QUOTA-M1-02; the build's ledger, QD-937).

521. **`FlushServiceDebits` takes no claim share now; the financial shares are rebalanced once service debits carry production volume (option 1 now, option 2 later, the recommendation, 2026-11-15).**
    - Question: the four existing claim shares at period 8 (the legacy provider totals, their drain, `FlushTransferDebits`, `AdvancePayment`) already spend exactly the half of ordinary claim turns taskworker's scheduler allows, and a fifth share at any period would panic every taskworker at construction.
    - Answer: `FlushServiceDebits` takes no claim share in server/taskworker/claim_admission.go: its chains are registered production-only and run on the ordinary queue and their function's indexed lane, as built. Service debits ship dark at M2, so behind a totals backlog a shard's flush may wait about a minute per claim, which delays only the Postgres debit and the token release, never a refusal. Once service debits carry production volume (M5's tls leases at the earliest), the shares are rebalanced from a measurement: `FlushTransferDebits`, `AdvancePayment` and `FlushServiceDebits` at period 12 each, the totals lane and its drain at 8 (1/8 + 1/8 + 3/12 = 1/2), a retuning of production's financial shares made then, by the owner of those tunings (§20.6's flush row).
    - Cost: behind a totals backlog a flush may wait about a minute per claim, balances reading higher than they are for that long.
    - Not taken: (3) one shared lane for both debit flushes, a scheduler change outside the area.
    - Raised by the build (QUOTA-M2-03; the build's ledger, QD-943).

522. **Twelve readings of the quota path stand as built: the dark switch's rates, fail-open, the spent refusal, the storage limit, the relay meter, a balance's room, the draw order before M4, carves, the flush's releases, leaving spent, the workload profile and the over-limit cut (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-15).**
    - Question: §20.5 to §20.7 and the plan did not settle twelve points of CountRate's dark switch, the spent refusal, the storage limit, the usage apply and the debit flush.
    - Answer: (1) While `enforce_sidecar_limits` is off, a rate whose limit only pro.yml sets, with no floor (`evs_drops_per_day`, `evs_drop_previews_per_day`, `evs_read_receipts_per_repo_day`), neither counts nor refuses, with no Redis call; the closure rates, allowance and pacing and the erasure push class stay live, since spent is never dark. (2) A CountRate call Redis does not answer, or whose answer cannot be read, passes and counts `urnetwork_quota_rate_fail_open_total`; a caller whose own context ended gets that error. (3) An empty balance's refusal carries `limit_key` `data`, `upgrade` true, limit and used 0, for every cause. (4) A network is over storage once its count reaches the limit (count >= limit), so zero refuses the first upload; Team's limit is `storage_overage_max` while `storage_gib_month` is priced, or a larger `storage` a row sets, and `storage` while unpriced. (5) A usage batch naming meter `relay` is refused whole: relayed bytes settle by contract. (6) A balance's room is `balance_byte_count` less the unapplied rows of both journals, Redis reservations not subtracted; in-window means start_time <= now < end_time on the batch's caller's clock, depleted balances included, so bytes past every balance's room overdraw the last in-window balance in draw order. (7) Before M4, `LoadDrawOrderBalances` orders by (`grant_kind IS NOT DISTINCT FROM 'team_credit'`, end_time, start_time, balance_id) with no gate, identical to escrow's order until a `team_credit` balance exists. (8) A carve must name a network and meter the batch's events name, a meter's carves may not pass its event's transfer bytes, what they leave draws at apply, and a carve row already journaled under its (balance, usage id) is that carve. (9) At the flush, evs rows release nothing, relay rows release the contract id, their usage id, and tls, mx and smtp rows `svc/<usage_id>`; releasing a token that is gone is a no-op. (10) The 60 s pass clears a spent mark once an active in-window balance has room past its pending debits, Redis reservations aside, and a lease refused while Postgres shows room marks the network again. (11) The spent pass and the flush register in the production workload profile only. (12) An `OverLimit` answer past 200,000 networks (a setting) is cut and counted, the rest reading as not over (§20.5, §20.6, §20.7, §20.9).
    - Cost: none in code; the closure bounds stay live while the switch is off.
    - Not taken: (2) correcting any by number, the alternative to (1) darkening the closure bounds too, so spent networks' erasure runs unpaced against the HDD sets while the switch is off.
    - Raised by the build (QUOTA-M2-03; the build's ledger, QD-944).

523. **A network's downgrade clock starts when its entitlement ended, the end of the newest window of a balance that conferred the tier it left (option 2, the recommendation, 2026-11-15).**
    - Question: a reading had the downgrade clock start at the end of the network's newest renewal of the tier it left, but a Team's periods are granted by the job at its anchor, so a Team whose cycle invoice never pays kept its tier past that renewal's end and spent most of its 14 days while still Team.
    - Answer: `NetworkDowngradeTime` answers when the entitlement ended: the end_time of the newest window, already ended, of a `transfer_balance` that conferred the tier the network left (its `tier` column, MG6), for a Team its last `team` grant's end_time, the one-day grace included, and for Pro its last `pro` grant's. The balances are the entitlement's one source (D-QUOTA-1), so one rule serves Pro and Team: a Team whose cycle invoice never pays keeps `team` for the period `RefreshTeamTransferBalances` granted at its anchor plus the grace, and FS-51's 14 days of withholding, with DNS's effects, count from the moment `NetworkTierOf` drops, so "day 13 changes nothing" holds for Teams too. The function's signature is unchanged (§20.5, §20.7's downgrade clock).
    - Cost: the clock reads `transfer_balance` instead of `subscription_renewal`.
    - Not taken: (1) the renewal's end, a lapsed Team withheld at once on dropping; (3) two rules for one clock.
    - Raised by the build (QUOTA-M4-01; the build's ledger, QD-946, which replaces the reading QD-295).

524. **CountRate's key grammar stands as built, and the two fixed 60-a-minute erasure rates become pro.yml rows read with a floor of 60 (option 1, the recommendation, with its open point settled, 2026-11-15).**
    - Question: §20.7 and §11.12 gave an eraser's `?closure=1` uploads and a network's ERASE, CUT and CLOSURE pushes 60 a minute each with no pro.yml key, against R-QUOTA-5's limits only via pro.yml, and six points of the key grammar and the usage batcher were unsettled.
    - Answer: points 2 to 7 stand as built: the read-receipt key carries no day and the Quota windows it by its own clock; a class-only key is counted in a call of its own; a drop job's cursor range is two length-prefixed byte strings; a batch carries at most 1,000 events; and the pacing's refusal names `evs_closure_uploads_per_s` with its limit, used and `retry_after_ms`. Point 3, a refused debit id, stands as owner decision 516 built it, since that decision answered it on 2026-11-10 and the build follows it: a refusal records nothing, so a retry keeps its debit id. Point 1 is settled by R-QUOTA-5 and owner decision 297's settings rule: the two rates become rows of `sidecar_abuse:`, `evs_closure_uploads_per_min: 60` (an eraser's `?closure=1` uploads, §20.7) and `evs_erasure_pushes_per_min: 60` (a network's ERASE, CUT and CLOSURE pushes, §11.12), fixed like `evs_closure_uploads_per_s` (owner decision 146), which the grammar reads with a floor of 60, so no configured value slows erasure; their refusals name those keys (§20.4's yaml, §20.6, §20.7).
    - Cost: two pro.yml rows and the grammar's floor.
    - Not taken: (2) correcting any point by number; for point 1, the rates kept as fixed grammar rows with no pro.yml key, against R-QUOTA-5.
    - Raised by the build (QUOTA-M2-01; the build's ledger, QD-966).

## The build's owner questions on abuse, safety and legal process (2026-11-06 to 2026-11-15)

The design-records track for §21, §22 and §23 folded the answers below on 2026-11-16, each citing its ledger id: the owner-class items the owner accepted in bulk on 2026-11-06, the one it decided alone that day (QD-008), and the answers it decided one at a time on 2026-11-12 and 2026-11-15. A decision that groups several answers carries its latest answer's date. The section's other items, the defect fixes the owner accepted on 2026-11-06 (QD-067, QD-078, QD-088, QD-111, QD-194, QD-195) and the build's readings and fills, went into §21 and the sections they name as text with no number.

525. **A stranger's report of CSAM or a threat to life comes due in 4 business hours on every tier (option 2, the recommendation, 2026-11-06).**
    - Question: §21.4's row for CSAM and threat to life needs a person to read the report first, and §21.8 gave such a report no row of its own, so it fell to Other: 4 business hours on probation, 72 h paid, 7 days on Community, though §21.9's reporting duty runs from actual knowledge (18 U.S.C. §2258A(a)).
    - Answer: §21.8's fourth row takes both categories at 4 business hours on every tier, one line of the abuse worker's signal mapping; counsel is asked whether the weekend gap is acceptable, and the pager stays as owner decision 134 set it (§21.8, §21.17, §23.7).
    - Cost: reviewer time on false reports; a report filed on a Friday night still waits out the weekend.
    - Not taken: (1) the table as it stood, a threat to life against a Community network past probation waiting a week; (3) the same and paging the rota, which closes the weekend but lets anyone page both rota members at 03:00 through a public form, reopening owner decision 134.
    - Raised by the build (the ABUSE plan; the build's ledger, QD-006).

526. **A network's or client's deletion on a held network reads as done and copies the rows the hold covers in its own transaction (silent deferral, as proposed, 2026-11-06).**
    - Question: owner decision 133 protects `--now`, the 7-day timer and per-entry erasure, not account deletion, so a §2703(f) target could delete its account the day a hold lands, while refusing the deletion would reveal a sealed hold, the reason owner decision 184 rejected refusing drops.
    - Answer: `RemoveNetwork` and `RemoveNetworkClient` on a held network read as done to the user and, in the deletion's own transaction, copy the in-scope rows they delete (api-1, api-4, api-5, `network_acl_head`) into the hold's `copied_rows`, with no refusal, hint or admin event. The repo shreds a deletion starts still run under owner decision 133, and the idle client reaper, a TTL sweep, skips held rows. Counsel sees this beside owner decision 133's condition (§21.9, R-ABUSE-5, §2.8's api-14, §23.7).
    - Cost: a hook in the deletion's transaction; identity rows outlive an account until the hold ends.
    - Not taken: (2) treating account deletion as a user's shredding, so a target erases its identity rows and counsel must accept that; (3) refusing the deletion while a hold stands, which tells the user a hold exists, against owner decisions 93 and 184.
    - Raised by the build (the ABUSE plan; the build's ledger, QD-008, which the owner decided alone on 2026-11-06).

527. **A hold's scope is the network or 1 to 16 repo ids (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-06).**
    - Question: `scope` stood in §21.9's table, api-14, evs-19 and §11.13, and no section gave its values, while R-ABUSE-5 says holds name accounts and a narrow order may name one repo.
    - Answer: `scope` is the string `network` or a list of 1 to 16 repo ids. A repo scope covers that repo's objects on every cluster and its gkm group, and server rows (tls-3, mx-2, smtp-3, connect-1's and api's) are covered at the network whatever the scope. Counsel sets the scope from the order, `network` by default. Scopes compare by containment, a repo staying until the latest `expire_time` of any hold that covers it, and a reader takes an unknown or unparsed scope as the whole network (§21.9, §11.8, §2.8's api-14 and evs-19).
    - Cost: repo-scoped readers parse a list.
    - Not taken: (2) always the whole network, readers ignoring the field, so a hold on one repo keeps everything, which may exceed what an order asks.
    - Raised by the build (the ABUSE plan; the build's ledger, QD-026).

528. **Strikes count the network's halts of any category and copyright notices per network, and a hit on a killed host adds no strike (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-06).**
    - Question: §21.4 gave no rule for which halts count toward a suspension, a copyright count's unit or what a new hit on a host already killed adds, and each decides when a network is suspended.
    - Answer: readings 3, 5 and 7 of the abuse policy stand as landed. "3 halts" counts the network's `mail_halt`, of any category, never a burner label's, and §14.8's rule reads it the same way. Both copyright counts are per network, as a repeat-infringer policy is per subscriber (17 U.S.C. §512(i)), so a second valid notice about another host kills the host it names; "valid" is any copyright row not lifted as wrong, "upheld" a case in review state `upheld`. While a kill stands on a host, a threat case adds nothing and a copyright notice is forwarded with no second kill; a kill that ended or was lifted stops this. Counsel reads the copyright counts beside §23.7's list (§21.4, §14.8, §23.7).
    - Cost: three halts of mixed categories suspend a network.
    - Not taken: (2) counting per category or per host, so a network spreads abuse across categories or hosts and §512(i)'s policy reads weaker; (3) counting every hit on a killed host, so an hourly Web Risk pass over a stale listing climbs to a suspension with no new act by the customer.
    - Raised by the build (ABUSE-M5-04; the build's ledger, QD-033).

529. **Holds copy connect-1's contract rows at placement and leave the 7-day logs outside (option 1 of each, the recommendations, 2026-11-06).**
    - Question: §21.9 listed connect-1 in what a production reaches and said TTL sweeps skip held rows, but connect-1's reaper, its close-evidence deletes and its weekly partition drops have no per-network rule; and §21.9 named evs-7 while `copied_rows` listed only partition rows and Redis soft state, a log store having no sweep to skip.
    - Answer: connect-1 joins `copied_rows`. At placement the hold's copy takes the `transfer_contract`, `contract_close` and `contract_close_report` rows naming a client of the held network that exist then, as compressed parts; the contract horizon reaper, the close-evidence horizon and the weekly partition drops first ask for pending copies and wait only while a hold placed before their range's end has an unfinished copy, so no keep rule is added and every drop stays whole. evs-7's and ops-1's logs, which go at 7 d, stay outside holds, though a production reaches them while they are retained. Counsel sees connect-1's rows beside §23.7's item (§21.9, §2.8's api-14 and connect-1, §23.7).
    - Cost: the largest copy a hold makes, one busy network's 35 to 91 days of contracts possibly millions of rows; a hold keeps none of the last 7 days of request logs.
    - Not taken: for contracts, (2) a per-network keep rule that blocks a partition drop while a held network has rows in its week, pinning every network's rows for up to a year, and (3) leaving connect-1 outside holds, narrowing owner decision 133's scope; for the logs, copying evs-7's lines at placement, a scan of 7 days of every network's logs per hold, since ops-1 carries no network label.
    - Raised by the build (the ABUSE plan; the build's ledger, QD-036, QD-133).

530. **Registrar, registry and law-enforcement notices reach the rota through a company mailbox and `notice_senders`, and only the rota kills (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-06).**
    - Question: §21.8 listed the channels and the 1 h clock but no arrival path; role mail is parsed for headers or dropped unread (owner decision 78), so a notice mailed to abuse@ was never read, and a machine kill on unauthenticated text would let anyone kill a customer's host.
    - Answer: both routes. Registrar, registry and law-enforcement contacts name a company mailbox off the platform, which the owner names (legal@ur.io, say), whose provider rule fires the page, and the rota files the case through the staff route; `abuse.yml` gains `notice_senders`, domain to class, so mail to abuse@ or postmaster@ whose DKIM verifies under a listed domain pages both rota members and opens a case, reading no body. No mail or form text drives a kill: §21.8's machine host kill for a registrar's or registry's URL becomes a rota action inside the same 1 h, and the public form offers no registrar or law-enforcement class (§21.8, §21.16, D-ABUSE-3, D-ABUSE-5, §23.7).
    - Cost: a sender list to keep, a third party holding notices, a paging path outside the pager service's own, and a person's kill where a machine's stood.
    - Not taken: (2) the DKIM sender list alone, missing an unlisted registrar; (3) the mailbox alone, losing a notice sent to abuse@; (4) private intake URLs per registrar, which registrars will not use.
    - Raised by the build (the ABUSE plan; the build's ledger, QD-038).

531. **A §21 suspension is one fan-out of server state that refuses new contracts for sidecar subjects only and leaves evs and gkm open (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-06).**
    - Question: §21.7 said "no reach, publishing or sending; data kept, readable and exportable", while §8.6 had evs refuse "as for §21's halts" and §8.8's `Suspend` needs a customer's signature.
    - Answer: a §21 suspension is server state feeding §8.6's `hardDeny`, never a signed `Suspend` (I-4). One `abuse_action` fans out: `network_abuse_state = suspended`; a `*` kill; `SuspendZone`, so MX goes and senders retry; a mail halt from M9; every open contract closed; new contracts refused for sidecar subjects only, shares included; no `rev/` entry; evs and gkm untouched, so reads, writes, erasure and export continue; certificates kept unless the case orders revocation, so a lift restores service at once. §8.6's sentence no longer reaches evs under a suspension (§21.7, §21.10, §8.6).
    - Cost: a suspended network keeps evs writes, and its ordinary VPN clients keep working.
    - Not taken: (2) every contract refused and `rev/` written for every subject, cutting a paying VPN user over a sidecar case and forcing re-enrollment after a lift; (3) only the hard deny and the kill, each gate deciding for itself, so gates disagree and §21.10's restore has no single action to re-apply.
    - Raised by the build (the ABUSE plan; the build's ledger, QD-049).

532. **Abuse staff act through one `x-internal` route on per-person bearers hashed in vault, with `sidecarctl abuse` as their client (option 1 of each, the recommendations, 2026-11-15).**
    - Question: §21.16's one staff route covered only actions and limit rows, while §21.8 to §21.10 give the abuse role lookups, reviews, appeals, holds and restore work with no route; the access log and the second-reviewer rule need a credential naming a person; and the client the plan named, `bringyourctl abuse`, had no task once operations commands moved to `sidecarctl` (owner decision 274).
    - Answer: one staff route, `POST /abuse/action`, `x-internal`, its kind covering lookup, review, an appeal's decision, a limit row, a hold's placement and renewal, a legal request, a production's sign-off, a restore flag's clear and a mailed action's re-apply. Each staff member holds a bearer of their own, kept hashed in vault's `abuse-staff.yml` as {reviewer_id, sha256, roles (reviewer, legal, engineering), expires}, at most 90 days old, compared in constant time; the lb refuses the route from outside the operators' addresses; each read writes `abuse_access_log` before it answers, an appeal's reviewer differs from the action's, and a production needs one legal and one engineering id. "Separate from ops" stays a process rule in v0, since nothing stops someone holding vault or Postgres. The client is `sidecarctl abuse <kind> [flags]`, in server/sidecarctl/cmd_abuse.go, one subcommand per kind, posting `{kind, ...}` to the route with the bearer read from a file a flag or `UR_ABUSE_STAFF_BEARER_FILE` names, never an argument, and printing the answer's JSON, the api URL from a flag that defaults to the env `WARP_ENV` names; later kinds add a subcommand each, and the role keeps a command-line client and no web UI (§21.10, §21.16, §19.4).
    - Cost: a vault file and its rotation, and one lb rule; ops can still read the file.
    - Not taken: for the route, (2) staff signing in as allowlisted URnetwork users on a fresh admin token, so a compromised login reaches abuse reads, and (3) no route, a client on an ops host under a Postgres role, so a 03:00 kill needs a host login; for the client, another lane writing the same command after the route landed.
    - Raised by the build (the ABUSE plan and ABUSE-M5-11; the build's ledger, QD-126, QD-896).

533. **Role mail no report attributes is refused at the end of DATA with the form's link, and no auto-reply is sent (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-06).**
    - Question: §21.8 named an auto-reply and no sender for it, the platform listener holds no mail credential, and mx-4 keeps the envelope in memory.
