Owner decision 606's other rules are unchanged: with no destination and route local traffic on, default traffic still stays off the tun, since there is no exit to send it to, and with route local traffic off it is captured and refused. Networks without the lock leave the choice to each person, and the field is additive to the format (QD-1053). The `egress_change` `CanonicalAction` gains `bool require_default_egress = 41`, set only by `egress_change` under `exits`, optional in kind 5's row and required by no template. Approvers render it as its own clause, "and require its devices to keep default egress on"; the action's check refuses it under `auto` or `none`; and an approver built before it refuses the action, since the parse refuses a field it cannot name, the closed direction. A filing with the requirement and one without are different actions, and the gate never reuses one's decision for the other; `ur bridge egress --require-default-egress` files it (QD-1060). It extends owner decision 462's fields (§17.8's `BridgeEgress` and its fence, §17.11, §16.8's `CanonicalAction` fence, §8.10).
    - Cost: a sealed field and an action field, both additive; an approver's screen gains a clause.
    - Not taken: for QD-1053, (2) leaving default egress always the person's choice; for QD-1060, carrying the requirement only in the sealed statement, which an approver would not see and a gate would not bind.
    - Raised by the owner's follow-up to owner decision 606 and the build (the ur-tun-default-egress-option track; the build's ledger, QD-1053 and QD-1060).

608. **Presence keys verify no WebAuthn attestation: clients request attestation none, `KeyRegister.attestation` stays in the format empty, and every presence key shows one label, presence (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-17).**
    - Question: owner decisions 327, 455 and 553 had the registering device verify a presence key's attestation over the create ceremony's client data against vendor roots each release pins, label each key attested, asserted or attestation refused, and disclose in api-7 the attestation object with the authenticator's model and its vendor's certificate chain, for a label that changes no authorization; the complexity review asked whether the verifier, its vectors, the pinned root list and its release step and the labels earn their cost.
    - Answer: presence keys no longer verify WebAuthn attestation. Clients request attestation none; `KeyRegister.attestation` stays in the format, empty; and every presence key shows one label, presence. The key's own proof in `KeyRegister`, which the log checks (QD-086), is unchanged. In the review phase the attestation verifier (record_presence_attest*), its vectors, the pinned vendor-root list and its release step, and the attestation refused and asserted labels are removed. §23.7's counsel item on the FIDO metadata terms is dropped, and §2.8's api-7 row no longer discloses the authenticator model or the vendor's chain. It reverses owner decisions 327, 455 and 553, and the answers to QD-137, QD-730, QD-885, QD-924 and QD-983 (§16.6's register, labels and registration rows; §7.9's `KeyRegister`; §18.5's `SidecarPresenceSigner`; §2.8's api-7, list v56; §23.7's counsel).
    - Cost: a future attested label on approver screens is lost; nothing about authorization changes, and servers see less.
    - Not taken: (2) keeping attestation as owner decisions 327, 455 and 553 had it.
    - Raised by the complexity review of 2026-11-17 (the build's ledger, QD-1035).

609. **Remote search stays and C19's window accounting goes: the remote-search pair's contracts are accounted like any peer's, with no window ledger, floor, withheld reports, sequence moves at a window's end, bit-1 marking, keep-warm pairs or keepalive (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-17).**
    - Question: owner decisions 176, 188 and 191 had connect change C19 account a requester-indexer pair in 55-minute windows, withholding each direction's reports to the window's end and reporting a 256 KiB floor, so api's stored rows showed up to five searches through an indexer as none, while relays still saw each search live; a keepalive a minute held the pair's sequences open in the foreground, owner decision 391 kept two own pairs warm, and owner decision 268 had every Gn-out merge of connect wait for FS-125's sweep. The complexity review counted about 1.4k lines and 3k test lines in connect, 0.5k and 1.2k in sidecar, PROTO-M10-05, the sweep every connect merge waited on, and 512 KiB of transfer balance a warm pair for each 55 foreground minutes.
    - Answer: remote search stays (owner decisions 175 and 178), and C19's window accounting goes. The remote-search pair's contracts are accounted like any peer's: there is no window ledger, no withheld reports or floors, no moving of sequences at window ends, no Hello bit 1 marking of the pair, and no keep-warm pairs or their pings. The fixed body sizes that hide searches from relays stay. api's retained rows now show when a phone searched through an indexer and how many 20 KiB exchanges it took, the same that and when evs-26 concedes for "search more"; no query, hit or repo is exposed. It reverses owner decisions 176, 188 and 191, owner decision 391's warm pairs, whose first two own indexers asked at once stand, and owner decision 268's gate, under which FS-125's sweep blocked every connect merge from M1's on, with the answers to QD-150, QD-385, QD-427 and QD-929. PROTO-M10-05 (FS-125) and QUOTA-M10-02 (the windows in relayed settlement) are dropped and held, and FS-83 loses its connect clause; FS-125's row keeps its number and gates while sidecartest/fault's catalog holds it (§4.6's C19, §4.8, §4.17, §4.18; §6.10, §6.11's bit 1, D-PROTO-22, Q-PROTO-4, §6's risks and phasing; §2.8's connect-5, list v57, and §2's relay row; §3's flow; §11.14's order, failover and remote rows; §18.4, §18.7, D-SDK-27; §20.6; §22.7, FS-83, FS-125; §23.6).
    - Cost: api's rows show when a phone searched through an indexer and how many exchanges it took, as evs-26 shows "search more"; a search that finds no session to its indexer waits for one Hello.
    - Not taken: (2) deferring remote search entirely; (3) keeping both.
    - Raised by the complexity review of 2026-11-17 (the build's ledger, QD-1038).

610. **v0 distributes every group key through §10.5's `KeyDelivery` wraps alone, and MLS is Later, its drafted code dark on the branch (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-17).**
    - Question: owner decision 1 answered Q-KEYS-9 with "MLS ships in v0", and wraps have since grown into a full distributor, serving agents (owner decision 114), member devices past the first 1,000 (owner decisions 144, 182), Delegations (owner decision 197) and recovery codes. The complexity review counted MLS's cost: about 42.5k MLS-only lines, 15.9k of them production, about 23 decisions, 98 KB of DESIGN-DETAILS.md section 6, about 14 of KEYS' 36 tasks and 27-63 MB of heap at 1,000 leaves (QD-641); and it imposed limits, an agent's repo keyless until a member device forms its group and recovery above 16 leaves waiting for a leaf online.
    - Answer: v0 distributes every group key (RK_g) through §10.5's `KeyDelivery` wraps alone, and MLS is set aside for Later. A join is a LATE delivery, a removal the existing REMOVE rotation, and a resync fetches deliveries; gkm drops epochs, Welcomes, KeyPackages, GroupInfo and acks, so `gkm:<group_id>` is versioned by generation alone, and the format's epoch fields stay, empty. The leaves rule (owner decisions 144 and 182) and owner decisions 361, 363 and 366 become moot, and so does QD-641's MLS engine rewrite. The drafted MLS code (the root's `mls_*`, mlsinterop, gkm's MLS files, gkmservice's epochs, groupinfo, keypackages, welcomes, acks and reform, and sidecartest's fake_mls, mlssuite and bench_groups) stays on the branch, dark, for Later, together with owner decision 99's deferred audit. The review phase reworks key formation (KF1 to KF7) onto deliveries. The MLS interop freeze (KEYS-M2-11's 0x0010), fuzzing of the `mls_*` decoders, and the FS-15, -20, -24, -97 and -104 MLS legs leave the gates. MLS's design stays as the design it returns with, and DESIGN-DEFER.md's keys-mls entry documents the deferral. It reopens Q-KEYS-9, amended to wraps alone, and reverses owner decision 1's "MLS ships in v0" and owner decision 60, whose one group per repo returns with MLS (§10.4, §10.5, §10.7, §10.9, D-KEYS-1, -2, -3, -12, -14, -18 and -23, Q-KEYS-9, §10.16; §7.9's leaves row; §5.6's reads row; §23.4).
    - Cost: TreeKEM's post-compromise healing of the key-encryption keys is lost: a copied keychain heals by §10.5's bounds instead, a reader at its ReaderKey's not_after plus the next generation, where a leaf healed in about 37 days (60 above 50 leaves). Each rotation costs about 1.37 KB a holder instead of one 72 B leaf wrap a leaf: about 1.37 MB and 1,000 objects at 1,000 holders. Gained: no 1,000-leaf trees on phones; an agent's repo is keyed without waiting for a member device to form a group; and recovery above 16 leaves needs no leaf online.
    - Not taken: (2) keeping MLS with wrap-only config and directory groups; (3) keeping MLS as planned.
    - Raised by the complexity review of 2026-11-17 (the build's ledger, QD-1040).

## The build's answers of 2026-11-18, folded by a second late sweep

The design-records track that swept up the answers recorded after the first late sweep numbered these, in the order it folded them. Each is a question the build raised and the owner answered on 2026-11-18, one at a time, accepting the recommendation under the owner's standing acceptance of 2026-11-16.

611. **The mail breakers read all four of §21.5's measures: Google Postmaster Tools and Microsoft SNDS under vault credentials of their own, each with a §2.8 row, and the listings from a second copy of the feed mirror on taskworker's host (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-18).**
    - Question: ABUSE-M9-03 built three sources for the breakers, each answering unknown with no credential: a Postmaster Tools client for `<root>`'s daily statistics, an SNDS client for the shared sending addresses' data file, and a listing client that matches URnetwork's own sending addresses against the feed mirror locally. But §2.8 had no row for Google's or Microsoft's requests, neither credential existed, the mirror sat on mxedge's hosts out of taskworker's reach, taskworker did not read `smtpedge.yml`'s sending addresses, and nothing said how to read a statistics answer with no spam ratio, a field the API's JSON drops when it is zero, so the breakers ran on the feedback-loop complaint rate alone.
    - Answer: the mail breakers read all four of D-ABUSE-2's measures. §2.8 gains two rows: ext-14, Google Postmaster Tools, which sees URnetwork's request for `<root>`'s daily statistics, and ext-15, Microsoft SNDS, which sees URnetwork's request for its own addresses' file; neither names a network. Their credentials are vault files, `abuse-postmaster-tools.yml`, a service account's refresh credential, and `abuse-snds.yml`, the key, which the owner creates and provides (§21.16, §23.7). taskworker reads `smtpedge.yml`'s sending addresses and installs the sources at start. The listing comes from a second copy of the feed mirror, synced to taskworker's host by the same pull, ext-4's row amended to name taskworker. An absent ratio reads as unknown, never as zero, so such a day holds a level rather than counting toward its clearing (§21.5's measures, §21.16, D-ABUSE-2; §15.4's listings; §19.4's taskworker row; §2.8's ext-4, ext-14 and ext-15, list v58; §23.7).
    - Cost: two outside services that see URnetwork's own requests for its own domain and addresses, two vault credentials and the owner's steps to create them, and a second copy of the mirror; a day whose ratio was exactly zero holds a breaker instead of counting toward its clearing.
    - Not taken: (2) reading an absent ratio in a 200 answer for the asked day as zero, so a quiet `<root>` clears on time, trusting a field that was not sent; (3) running the breakers on the complaint rate and the listings alone, so level 1's first trigger and SNDS red never fire and the clearing rule must ignore the missing measures.
    - Raised by the build (the abuse-m5-10 track; the build's ledger, QD-1069).

612. **api and taskworker each page §21.8's notices through a retrying sink over monitor's pager webhook, installed at start, `sidecarctl abuse file_notice` files a notice, and the role-mail intake reaches `FileAbuseNoticeOfMail` through an internal api route carrying header facts only (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-18).**
    - Question: ABUSE-M5-10 built the staff route's notice filing, the filing of a role mail whose DKIM verified under a `notice_senders` domain (owner decision 530) and taskworker's clock pass, all paging through one seam whose default refuses every page, so that no notice reads as paged before a real sink is there. But only monitor built a pager sink, from its own vault file; the listeners that verify role mail's DKIM handed §21 nothing and run in a block with no case tables in reach; and no `sidecarctl` command filed a notice.
    - Answer: api and taskworker each read the pager webhook's settings from the vault file monitor reads, build `pager.NewRetrySink(pager.NewWebhookSink(...))` and install it with `controller.SetAbuseNoticePager` at start. `sidecarctl abuse file_notice` is added. The role-mail intake reaches `FileAbuseNoticeOfMail` through an internal api route, service token only, carrying the recipient's local part, its root, and each DKIM signature's d= and verdict, never a body. The page leaves the process that committed the case, and the clock pass covers a crash between the two. There are two more readers of the pager credential (§21.8's intake, §21.10's staff route, §21.16; §19.4's api and taskworker rows, §19.8; §2.8's ext-13, list v59).
    - Cost: two more readers of the pager credential; an internal route and a command.
    - Not taken: (2) monitor alone paging, on a gauge of open notice cases under one fixed key, which keeps one holder of the credential but loses the case id, waits a scrape interval and cannot dedupe per notice, so a second notice inside an open page wakes nobody; (3) paging staff-filed notices only, leaving owner decision 530's mail route unwired.
    - Raised by the build (the abuse-m5-10 track; the build's ledger, QD-1070). It follows owner decisions 498 and 530.

613. **`mail_send` gains an optional `uses`, by which an agent asks for a multi-use template of its alias and recipients, and every other kind stays single-use (option 2, the recommendation, 2026-11-18).**
    - Question: owner decision 574 gave `ApprovalRequest` the uses its agent asks for a template, and the daemon files that count and refuses one above 1 for anything but a template, but no O tool in §16.5's catalog built a template or took a count: each builds one action, so every MCP filing named one use, and §16.6's multi-use row, with the repeat-mail template the approved-recipients plan's interim presumes (QD-034), was out of an agent's reach. Adding an argument changes §16.5's static catalog (D-MCP-3), so it was the owner's to decide.
    - Answer: `mail_send` gains an optional `uses`. With uses of 2 or more, the daemon builds a template from the call's `from_alias` and its recipients, to, cc and bcc as the allowed set, with no body hash or preview, so each later `mail_send` from that alias to a subset spends a use (`ActionMatchesTemplate`). The call answers `approval_pending` with the template's id, and the agent retries its first mail once the template is approved. This serves QD-034's repeat-mail template. Every other kind stays single-use, no other O tool taking a count, and the count is the daemon-built field owner decision 574 renders and signs (§16.5's catalog; §16.6's multi-use row; §16.8's `scope`).
    - Cost: `mail_send`'s schema and catalog entry, a mail template builder among `NewCanonicalAction`'s rows, MCP tests and §16.5's text.
    - Not taken: (1) no MCP surface for templates until a design gives one, so the multi-use row and the repeat-mail template stay out of an agent's reach; (3) an optional `uses` on every O tool whose kind can be a template, each kind's template naming which of its fields stay blank, a per-kind design with render text §16.6 does not give.
    - Raised by the build (the approval-request-uses track; the build's ledger, QD-1071). It follows owner decision 574.

614. **An `egress_change` template binds `require_default_egress` whether it sets it or not, so a template that leaves it empty covers only filings that leave it unset (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-18).**
    - Question: owner decision 607 put the requirement of default egress in an action field of its own, 41, so every approver sees it and the gate never reuses one filing's decision for the other, but §16.6's multi-use row binds a template by its non-empty fields alone, and a proto3 false is empty, so a two-use template of "exits through X", which its approver saw with no requirement, could spend its second use on the filing that also locks the subject's devices to default egress: the gap owner decision 607 closed, open again for templates through the gate's generic path, though nothing in this build files an `egress_change` template.
    - Answer: an `egress_change` template binds `require_default_egress` whether it sets it or not. A template of "exits through X" that leaves the requirement empty covers only filings that also leave it unset, so an `egress_change` template under `exits` covers one distinct action and is no wider than a single-use approval of that action. No decision the gate can spend, a template's included, spends on a filing whose requirement its approver did not see, which keeps QD-1060's promise. This is built: kind 5's row names field 41 as one a template binds, and `ActionMatchesTemplate` holds a use to the template's value of each such field, empty included; it only narrows, and it changes no format, field or other kind (§16.6's multi-use row; §16.8's `CanonicalAction` fence, field 41's comment).
    - Cost: none in code, which is built; a clause in §16.6 and a fence comment.
    - Not taken: (2) §16.6's rule as written, a template that leaves the requirement empty covering filings with it and approvers rendering the open requirement as a clause of its own; (3) refusing `egress_change` templates altogether, amending owner decision 462's row, for no gain over (1).
    - Raised by the build (the egress-lock-action-field track; the build's ledger, QD-1073). It follows owner decision 607.

615. **A policy's own `ip:` deny test fails at save where a route rule that names no prefix takes its source and names, for it, a sidecar among both its via subjects and its subject destinations, the failure naming that rule (option 2, the recommendation, 2026-11-18).**
    - Question: owner decision 575 has a route rule that names no `ip:` destination and no `autogroup:internet` reach, through its destination vias, every prefix of their subnet routes in force, which are ledger facts that change after a save, while a policy's own tests, run on the writer's device before signing, judged an `ip:<cidr>#route` check by each rule's prefixes alone, so such a rule met neither an accept nor a deny: under Team's grant 1 a member's deny of 10.0.0.0/8 passed at every save, though once one of the member's devices filed a route of 10.1.0.0/16 the member's other devices reached it, and a deny test no longer bounded what the head could route to.
    - Answer: a policy's own `ip:` deny test fails at save where a route rule that names no prefix takes the source and names, for it, some sidecar that is both among its via subjects and among its subject destinations, since that sidecar's routes in force could serve any range. The failure names that rule. An accept of such a range still fails. Under grant 1, every member `ip:` deny test then fails until the Team narrows grant 1, by an `ip:` destination or no `route`, so a deny test again bounds what the head can route to. The answer places this in §8.6; DESIGN.md states a policy's tests in §8.8's saving rules, so it goes there, with a pointer from §8.4's reading of such a rule (§8.8's Saving paragraph; §8.4's readings).
    - Cost: a few lines in `routeToPrefix` and its tests; a Team whose members keep `ip:` deny tests narrows grant 1 before its next save.
    - Not taken: (1) tests judging a rule's prefixes alone, said in §8.8, so a deny test reads as a bound it is not for any document with such a rule; (3) refusing such an `ip:` test as one no test can judge, with a validation message in place of a failed test, which refuses accepts too.
    - Raised by the build (the bridge-prefixless-route-self track; the build's ledger, QD-1072). It follows owner decision 575.

616. **A client generation that cannot start because a file of the subprotocol store fails decoding is §5.9's Failed: the sidecar enters Locked, or stays Offline with a reason, and raises `refused` once, a later wake retrying (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-18).**
    - Question: when a client generation's start failed, the core logged it and returned, leaving the sidecar Offline with no client, no event and no state reason until a wake that nothing schedules, so a sealed file in the network's subprotocol store that failed decoding, the store that holds §6.8's push versions beside the pins, kept the sidecar Offline for good with nothing a host or `ur status` could show. The keychain kill test's in-place mutant reached it, in 9 of 627 restarts under -race and load; on the real store, where every write renames a synced temporary file, it means disk damage or a foreign write, which §5.9 treats as Failed for the key stores, while its class rows for component state did not say which applies to this store.
    - Answer: a generation that cannot start because a subprotocol-store file fails decoding is §5.9's Failed. The sidecar enters Locked, or stays Offline with a reason, and raises `refused` once, so a host and `ur status` see why it is not online. A later wake retries, as now. This keeps the no-spin property: one event, then a wait for a wake. Giving the subprotocol store's files a §5.9 class, so that what is derived can be rebuilt, can follow if the review phase finds files there that are safe to rebuild (§5.9's store states; §5.4's state reason).
    - Cost: one event and a state or reason on a path that only logged.
    - Not taken: (2) a §5.9 class for the subprotocol store's files now, dropping and rebuilding what is derived and alarming only for what holds pins, which needs a decision on which of its files carry rollback protection; (3) a log line alone, so a sidecar silently never comes online after store damage.
    - Raised by the build (the keychain-kill-test-load track; the build's ledger, QD-1074).

## The build's answers of 2026-11-18 and 2026-11-19, folded by a third late sweep

The design-records track that swept up the answers recorded after the second late sweep numbered these, in the order it folded them. Each is a question the build raised and the owner answered on 2026-11-18 or 2026-11-19, one at a time, accepting the recommendation under the owner's standing acceptance of 2026-11-16. A decision that groups several answers carries its latest answer's date. Three more answers of those days are accepted defect fixes and went in as text, with no number: a gate's list check runs nothing, the operations matrix's commands included (§23.8; the build's ledger, QD-1082); §6.10 and §23.9 lose the two sentences that still described C19 as live after owner decision 609 (QD-1083); and a store's adapter answers an ended context as an error, never re-raising it into an engine's own goroutine (§4.5; QD-1090).

617. **Under a flood the P1 shed refuses only networks strictly heavier than the lightest one waiting, and admission is fair over time (option 3, the recommendation, 2026-11-18).**
    - Question: a process's budget admitted a P1 write at once while its queue was empty, so a flood arriving early in an interval spent the process's whole A1 share, and every push that asked after it waited for the next share, 5 s on. Past the shed's delay the shed refused the heaviest network and kept refusing the next heaviest, never the last one waiting, breaking equal loads by id: with a flood and two Community networks queued, one of the two light pushes was refused too. FS-81's "the others keeping their p99", and FS-61's flood clause, held only for one light network at a time, and FI-14 (d) says a network is refused only while one with less queued priced bytes per weight is served.
    - Answer: the shed refuses only networks strictly heavier than the lightest network waiting, so equal light loads are kept, and among equal loads it refuses by id only when every waiting network is equally loaded past the delay, so a uniform overload still sheds. Admission is also fair over time: an immediate admission advances its network's virtual finish tag as a queued one does, and a network past its weighted share of the interval's P1 queues behind the others even while the queue is empty, so a flood cannot spend the share before the light networks' pushes arrive. A light network is never refused while the flood holds more than its weighted share, and its pushes are served within the interval (§11.13's queue and shed rows; FI-14 (d)).
    - Cost: the queue and its client change, a little state per network per process, and FS-61's flood test run again.
    - Not taken: (1) the shed and admission as built, reading the clause as one light network at a time, so light networks that queue together behind a flood lose all but one push to 429s; (2) the shed's change alone, which fixes the tie but leaves light networks waiting 5 s behind every flood.
    - Raised by the build (the evsb-m10-08 track; the build's ledger, QD-1075).

618. **In a recovery, the groups step re-forms a live repo's group in two calls around its activate, and sends restore for a repo its state shows restored (option 1 of each question, the recommendation, 2026-11-18).**
    - Question: two gaps in the groups step owner decision 602 mends. For a live repo the step ran the place, the whole re-form and then activate; but while `repo/` reads keying, gkm admits only a group's form, epoch 0, ladder and first wraps (§10.6's checks row), so the re-form's rotation claim was refused, the step failed before its activate, and every rerun failed the same way. And a `RepoRestore` filed after the log resumed reaches a device's state seconds before evs's projection holds it: a groups step run in that window had evs's place route create `repo/` deleted, the device re-form a group gkm then refused, and nothing but taskworker's daily reconciler mend it.
    - Answer: a live repo's groups step re-forms its group in two calls around its activate. The first takes the form, epoch 0 and the ladder alone, which is all gkm takes while `repo/` reads keying or deleted; then activate, which finds the GroupForm and gen/1; then the re-form's resume from the keychain's record, which rotates and adds now that `repo/` is active, as a restored repo's second call does. Members return by the rotation and the Adds a little after `repo/` goes active, and §10.6's checks row stays as written. For a repo its state shows restored, a `RepoDelete` with a `RepoRestore` of it, the step also sends restore after the place and before the re-form: the route alone, with no new statement, taking 409 REPO_KEYING as the answer it expects. A 200 means `repo/` stood deleted and is now active under the projected `RepoRestore`, and the step waits out STATEMENT_MISSING while evs's projection trails, within a bound its settings hold, as owner decision 602's DELETE does (§19.10's groups row; §10.8's after row; §11.12's restore row).
    - Cost: members return a little after `repo/` goes active rather than before, and one refused restore for each restored repo a recovery re-seeds in the honest order. No format, route or state machine changes.
    - Not taken: for the re-form, (2) gkm admitting a re-form's first rotation claim, its wraps and the Adds' commits while `repo/` reads keying, a change to §10.6's checks row. For the restore, (2) evs's place route taking a retried place of such a `repo/` to active itself, which the device must run again after the projection catches up; (3) leaving it to the reconciler, so the repo's history and group return up to a day late.
    - Raised by the build (the reseed-mend-keying track; the build's ledger, QD-1076 and QD-1077). It extends owner decision 602.

619. **evs's DROP boundary rule takes a DROP push's §11.14 index rewrites beside its written lineages (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-18).**
    - Question: §11.14's drop row has a DROP push rewrite in place, without the listed versions, each index file holding a listed version of a dropped path, so the push carries changes to `.ur-index/` files beside its written boundaries. evs's boundary rule ended with "every change is a written lineage's: else 409 DROP_BOUNDARY", which refused any such rewrite, and nothing in §11.7 or §11.8 carved the rewrites out of it.
    - Answer: beside the written lineages' changes, evs's boundary rule takes changes of versioning-2 versions under an index root, `.ur-index/` on the DROP's own file-mode ref (`ur/index` is another ref and never rides the push), each a rewrite of a standing index file, never a creation or a removal, and holds them to §11.14's own rules: 5d's index_epoch and padding to the replaced length. A DROP push so carries the rewrites its drop row states, and a dropped version's postings do not outlive its content by the backfill's 10 minutes. A client keeps its resend without the rewrites on 409 DROP_BOUNDARY, for an older evs (§11.7's boundary row; §11.14's drop row).
    - Cost: a small change to the boundary check and its tests.
    - Not taken: (2) a DROP that never carries rewrites, the backfill rewriting within 10 minutes for every drop while searches mask the older versions.
    - Raised by the build (the evs2-m10-21 track; the build's ledger, QD-1078).

620. **A DROP whose index rewrites' upload is refused at the storage limit, while spent, or on a full metadata cluster is sent again once without them (option 1 of each question, the recommendation, 2026-11-19).**
    - Question: a rewrite of a slot, a large entry under 16 KiB, a head or a manifest is staged, but a rewrite of a bucket or all shard, up to 1 MiB, is an upload. At the storage limit or while spent, evs accepts a DROP that uploads no segment and refuses one that uploads, so a DROP whose kept head is inline but whose rewrites take a shard was refused for its index alone, though a person often drops history to get back under the limit. A metadata cluster at §19.9's cohort limit refuses the same uploads with 507 CLUSTER_FULL, which is not a QuotaRefusal, though §19.9's cohorts row lists drops among what passes there and a placed repo never moves.
    - Answer: a DROP that carried index rewrites and is refused for their upload, with a QuotaRefusal at the storage limit or while spent, or 507 CLUSTER_FULL on a metadata cluster at its cohort limit, is sent again once without its rewrites, left to the backfill, as after a second 409 INDEX_EPOCH. The DROP, which frees storage, is never blocked by its index. The listed versions' postings stay in the index files, masked by the kept version's newer entry (newest wins), until the backfill runs under the limit, or once the cluster has room: the gap §11.14 already accepts for a signer that does not hold the index. A refusal of the DROP without rewrites, its kept head's own upload, still goes to the person (§11.14's drop and limit rows; §19.9's cohorts row).
    - Cost: a dropped version's postings can outlive its content, masked, for as long as the limit holds.
    - Not taken: (2) the DROP refused whenever its rewrites upload a shard, so a DROP meant to free space fails for an index reason the person cannot see; (3) at the storage limit, a rewriter that builds only staged rewrites, which must learn the limit state before it builds and makes a DROP's rewrites differ by quota state.
    - Raised by the build (the search-m10-18 and drop-quota-resend-without-rewrites tracks; the build's ledger, QD-1088 and QD-1099). It follows owner decision 619.

621. **A host holding the text catches an in-ref push that changed its signer's head but left one of its documents out (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-19).**
    - Question: FS-76 asks that "a writer's delta that omits a document" be caught by hosts holding the text, and D-EVS-56 that such hosts re-extract each new document. The check judged only the entries a push added, so a document with no entry gave it nothing to judge, and §11.14's coverage row counts a push indexed once it changes its writer's head: a push that wrote two documents and entered one passed, no backfill re-entered it, and no search reported the omitted document as not yet searchable. Nothing said which index file's path the event names, since no file holds the missing entry.
    - Answer: for a push that changed its signer's head, a host with the text re-extracts every document the content push at that version changed, as the writer extracts it (a binary version, a path under the never row and a chunk D6 skips yield no doc_key), skipping what the erasure set at the push's epoch lists, and holds that the index at that version holds each resulting doc_key at that version or above, in any of the signer's files or the bucket's shards. A missing one is a mismatch: the host's own entry stands for it, the path is queued for a backfill, and `index_mismatch` names the signer and the head file the push wrote. The coverage row and every format stay as they are (§11.14's coverage row of its reads; D-EVS-56).
    - Cost: a host with the text pays one extraction per changed document, as it already does for the entries a push adds.
    - Not taken: (2) changing coverage, so a push counts indexed only when the index holds an entry or tombstone for every document it changed, which moves the cost to every reader of indexed_through, phones included, which hold no text; (3) leaving it to the review phase, so a writer can hide a document it commits from every search with no alarm.
    - Raised by the build (the search-m10-36 track; the build's ledger, QD-1098).

622. **taskworker's idle top-level marking holds off while its process has no good read of sidecar.yml (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-18).**
    - Question: owner decision 591 has taskworker's idle reap leave the clients of sidecar.yml's `urnetwork_edge` out of its idle marking. The reader answers the zero value for a first read of a file that is there but cannot be read or parsed, which closes every gate; for the marker the zero value is the destructive direction, since it names no `urnetwork_edge`. A taskworker that started during a broken push marked inactive each edge client whose slot no process had held for 30 days, the mark stuck, and the next deploy into that slot was refused its clients for up to 30 days.
    - Answer: the idle marking holds off while the process has no good read of sidecar.yml. The config records that it came from a failed first read, the reader's failed path with no previous read, and the reap marks nothing in such a run; its other passes run as before, and an absent file still lets the marker run, since the environment then has no `urnetwork_edge` to protect. The marker so fails in the direction that loses nothing, as the reader's gates do, and sidecar.yml stays the only source for the edge network (§13.6; §19.4).
    - Cost: ordinary idle clients wait for the first good read, minutes against a 30-day threshold.
    - Not taken: (2) a runbook step after every broken push, an operator deleting the rows the window marked; (3) the edge network's id in Postgres, a new column with its §2.8 row and a second record of which network is `urnetwork-edge`.
    - Raised by the build (the edge-idle-reap-skip track; the build's ledger, QD-1079). It follows owner decision 591.

623. **§2.8's dns-1 and dns-2 rows name a Team network's delegated `_domainkey.<domain>` zones (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-18).**
    - Question: a Team network whose verified domain delegates `_domainkey.<domain>` to the dns edges has its sending sidecars' selectors answered there, from one row of a new kind at the network's label apex. No column, Redis family, object family or proto field is added, so §22.7's schema guard does not fire, but the published list named no customer domain in dns's store or its query stream; api-3 already holds the domain.
    - Answer: dns-1's sees names each Team network's delegated `_domainkey.<domain>` apexes, kept with the verified domain's row, and dns-2's sees names a Team domain's delegated selector names among the query names an edge answers. The list takes its next version; no column, family or proto field changes. `delegated.enabled` may go on per environment once the rows are written and rollout ordering handles the kind (§2.8).
    - Cost: two phrases in the published list.
    - Not taken: (2) reading both rows as covering it already, so the published list never names customer domains in dns's store.
    - Raised by the build (the dns-m5-24 track; the build's ledger, QD-1080).

624. **A sidecar's calls and NO_REPLY sends reach an edge without §6.4's Hello, as its stream opens already do (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-18).**
    - Question: §14.5 makes `SmtpProof` and `SmtpHandshake` RPCs to the edge and `SmtpOutcome` a NO_REPLY envelope to the ticket's listener, and an edge carries no binding and drops every Hello. A call always verified its peer first, sending a Hello the edge never answers, so on a real stream every part's call failed at its deadline and nothing past the handshake reached the MX; and no method sent a NO_REPLY envelope to a peer, nor did the edge's dispatcher hand one to an engine.
    - Answer: a call marked for an edge (`CallOptions.Edge`, as `StreamOpenRequest.Edge`) runs no Hello and no HeadCheck, is refused for a peer the dispatcher knows as a sidecar, and has its answer admitted from a Service-mode source, as the inline guard already allows. `Subprotocol.SendEdge(clientId, messageType, body)` sends a NO_REPLY envelope under the same rule, and the edge role registers NO_REPLY handlers. The edge's identity is the signed `ur/` ticket the dialer verified, the basis its stream already rests on. No wire changes (§6.4's identity check; §14.5).
    - Cost: a small change to the subprotocol's caller side, with a test each.
    - Not taken: (2) carrying the parts and the handshake as stream frames ahead of the raw TLS, which changes §14.5's wire.
    - Raised by the build (the smtp-m9-07 track; the build's ledger, QD-1089).

625. **Each gate's sweep names every case whose T3 test runs outside server's sidecart3, and the gate convention's T3 entry carries the local stack's tag (option 1 of the first question; option 1 with option 3's check of the second, the recommendations, 2026-11-18).**
    - Question: a gate names each case's T3 test by convention in server's sidecart3, and reads a name missing if the package does not declare it under the convention's tags. Owner decision 548 named three cases in server's evshost. A scan found 23 more whose T3 tests run in evshost, server's controller, api and root packages, and FS-10's twins under names of their own, all reading missing. And every FS test in sidecart3 sits behind the local stack's build tag, which the convention's entry lacked, so a real gate run read every sidecart3 row missing while a list run read it listed.
    - Answer: as owner decision 548 did for three cases, each gate's exit sweep names the other 23 cases' T3 tests where they run, with the package's tags: M2's in evshost (FS-1 to FS-4, FS-6, FS-7, FS-12, FS-57, FS-58, FS-62, FS-63 and FS-113, and FS-10's twins by their real names), M4's in controller (FS-38, its forged case and FS-56), M6's in api (FS-19's team and invite cases and the team cases of FS-21, FS-97 and FS-124) and M10's (FS-72's migration case, FS-74 and FS-75). A T2+T3 case keeps its convention T2 test and no test moves. The convention's T3 entry carries `sidecar_localstack`, since a T3 Net hosts its stores on the local stack's MinIO, and the per-case sidecart3 entries two sweeps had added are dropped as redundant. A server test then checks that every test a sweep or the convention names is declared in its package under its tags, so a later rename or tag drift fails there (§22.8's gates line; §23.8's Gn-out).
    - Cost: 24 sweep entries in four files; every T3 row needs the local stack up at a gate run, which it needs anyway.
    - Not taken: for the other packages, (2) a catalog field for a case's T3 package and tags, a format change and a second mechanism beside owner decision 548's; (3) moving the tests into sidecart3, across six areas' files, which controller's internal tests cannot do without exporting seams. For the tag, (2) each milestone's sweep naming its sidecart3 cases one by one.
    - Raised by the build (the gate-sweep-evshost-rows and test-m2-19 tracks; the build's ledger, QD-1081 and QD-1085). It extends owner decision 548.

626. **A gate also runs each case's clause tests, registered against their row and case (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-18).**
    - Question: a case's tests are named by convention, a T2 test that runs the case's one registered builder and its T3 twin. Most rows run further schedules of the same case, their clauses, in tests the convention does not name, so a gate read a case pass on its row test alone and a failing clause never reached a gate report; and several rows had clause tests but no registered builder, reading missing however many clauses passed.
    - Answer: a clause registry. Each clause's schedule is registered against its row and case, and the gate convention names a case's clause tests too, by a derivable name, with T3 twins where a clause has one, so a gate reads a case pass only when its row test and every registered clause pass. Rows with clause tests but no registered builder get theirs. The generated gate list grows with the convention (§22.8's clauses line; §23.8's Gn-out).
    - Cost: a sweep across the areas' schedule tests to register clauses and align names, run late, when no running lane holds those files.
    - Not taken: (2) each milestone's sweep naming its cases' clause tests by hand, long lists that drift; (3) gates on the registered case alone, so a gate report can pass a case one of whose clauses fails.
    - Raised by the build (the test-m2-19 track; the build's ledger, QD-1084).

627. **M2's gate keeps FS-15, FS-20, FS-24 and FS-104 running their drafted schedules, their MLS legs counting toward nothing, until KEYS's review-phase rework moves the rows onto deliveries (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-18).**
    - Question: owner decision 610 takes the MLS interop freeze, the fuzzing of the `mls_*` decoders and the MLS legs of FS-15, FS-20, FS-24, FS-97 and FS-104 off the gates. §22.8's rows, §22.10's table, §23.6's M2 row, §23.8's freezes and D-KEYS-10 still put the 0x0010 interop and the four rows whole at M2's exit, and the fault catalog, held to §22.8's text, keeps the rows whole; the registered schedules mix MLS and delivery legs.
    - Answer: M2's gate keeps the four rows at M2 running their drafted schedules, with the sweep's open item saying their MLS legs count toward nothing, until KEYS's review-phase rework moves the rows onto v0's deliveries and design-records folds owner decision 610 into §22.8's four rows, §22.10's table, §23.6's M2 row, §23.8's freezes and D-KEYS-10 with it. The delivery legs' evidence stays visible, and the sweep says plainly what does not count. The epoch-retention resync test is not built, since v0 has no epochs or acks (§23.8's freezes; DESIGN-DEFER.md's keys-mls entry).
    - Cost: a failure in a dark MLS leg would fail an M2 row it no longer gates, and a pass there shows MLS code nobody ships, until the rework.
    - Not taken: (2) splitting each row's catalog cases now, an `mls` case gated Later, a §22.8 change ahead of the rework; (3) reading the four rows open at M2, so the gate shows no evidence for their delivery legs, which do ship.
    - Raised by the build (the test-m2-19 track; the build's ledger, QD-1086). It follows owner decision 610.

628. **The sdk binds the team directory's own repo id, so a directory view scopes its "search more" to the directory alone (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-18).**
    - Question: a phone keeps no directory buckets, and its directory "search more" asks a designated indexer that reads the directory. An app reached that only through a search over every repo: the query takes repo ids, and no bound type carried the directory's own repo id, which the core knows, so a view's "search more" would also fetch every other repo's all partition on its fallback.
    - Answer: `SidecarDirectory.GetRepoId() *Id` answers the directory's own repo id, nil while the network has none, and an app's directory view names it in `SidecarSearchQuery.RepoIds` with `Scope` "all". On the remote backend the daemon's directory status carries the id; until a control method does, it answers nil and the view's "search more" covers every repo, asking designated indexers first. One search entry point stays, and the bound-API gates are regenerated (§18.5; §18.4's directory row).
    - Cost: one bound method.
    - Not taken: (2) a second search entry point over the directory alone, which on the remote backend needs a daemon search argument naming the directory; (3) a query field that scopes to the directory, which every host must honor and which must be defined beside named repos.
    - Raised by the build (the sdk-m10-03 track; the build's ledger, QD-1087).

629. **A preservation hold binds a deleter from 5 s after api acknowledged its placement, the bound of §11.13's cache of `hold/` (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-19).**
    - Question: FI-16 says a versionless version's content is deleted only if no hold keeps it when the delete issues, and FI-27 that no hold covering the repo stood when the DELETE issued. Both mechanisms read holds through a cache: §11.13 has the purge and the drop job reread `hold/` "cached at most 5 s". A deleter following §11.13 exactly can so issue a DELETE up to 5 s after api acknowledged a hold, which the literal invariants would call a violation, and nothing in §21.9 said when a placed hold takes effect for deleters.
    - Answer: the invariants allow the cache. A hold binds a deleter, evs's purge and the drop job, from 5 s after api acknowledged its placement, the cache's bound, a settings field. api's placement says so, or reports the hold placed only once the bound has passed. The oracles stay as built, counting a hold from the cache's bound after its PUT was acknowledged; evs and taskworker change nothing (§21.9's preservation holds; FI-16; FI-27).
    - Cost: an object whose DELETE issues within 5 s of a hold's acknowledged placement is not kept by that hold.
    - Not taken: (2) the invariants read literally, every hold acknowledged before a DELETE binding it, which costs the purge and the job an uncached read of `hold/` before each DELETE, or a fence api raises, and changes both §11.13 rows.
    - Raised by the build (the test-m10-02 track; the build's ledger, QD-1091).

630. **§12.7's `_mta-sts` record is published only for a network with network_verified; the receivers' certificates join as a condition at the review phase (option 2 now and option 1's certificate condition at the review phase, the recommendation, 2026-11-19).**
    - Question: §15.5 has verified networks' receivers serve the MTA-STS policy once every receiver holds a §13 certificate, and §12.7 publish `_mta-sts`. The record was written for every zone with mail on: for an unverified network, whose policy no receiver may serve, which sends every MTA-STS sender a fetch that finds no listener; and for a network whose receivers are still on self-signed leaves, where a sender that cached the `mode: enforce` policy refuses an uncertified receiver.
    - Answer: `_mta-sts` is published only for a network with network_verified, as owner decision 96 names: the zone's mail policy carries it only for a verified network with mail on, and a verification change reconciles the zone. The short window in which a new receiver is still uncertified at M8 is accepted: the router's backup ticket and the sender's retry carry mail through it, and it fails only temporarily. At the review phase, once the receivers' issuance path settles, the record also waits until every published `mail_host_key` row for host `mx` has an unexpired certificate on the same key, a certificate's issuance and a host-key change reconciling the zone, so the record never names a policy an uncertified receiver would fail (§12.7; §15.5; DESIGN-DEFER.md's dns-mta-sts-certificate-gate entry).
    - Cost: one more caller of the zone's reconcile now, and a short window of temporary failures for each new receiver until the review phase's condition lands.
    - Not taken: (1) both conditions now, three new callers of the reconcile and one more join in it before the issuance path settles; (3) the record following mail on alone, which leaves both cases.
    - Raised by the build (the mail-m8-05 track; the build's ledger, QD-1092).

631. **A Team scope's own mailcfg repo is created by a consented device of the scope's member, and the access log's fold binds it to that member (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-19).**
    - Question: §15.7 gives each Team scope a mailcfg of its own, read only by its member's consented devices and receivers, and §8.5 has such a row name an owner. Nothing in the formats could make one: `RepoCreate` names no owner and no scope, §8.8 had a config writer create every mailcfg, the fold set an owner only for the owner-bound kinds, and no row names a scope's member. Every device took the first standing mailcfg in log order as the network's, so a scope's would be taken as the network's by any device that saw it first.
    - Answer: a scope's mailcfg is created by a consented device of the scope's member, and the access log's fold makes a mailcfg `RepoCreate` from a member device that is no config writer owner-bound to its creator's member, as a mailbox is: one a member, the first in log order. The network's mailcfg stays a config writer's creation with no owner. A device tells them apart by the row: the network's is the first standing mailcfg whose row names no owner, and a scope's the first whose row names that scope's member, so a scope's mailcfg is readable only by its member's consented devices by construction. No field is added (§8.8's RepoCreate row; §8.5's kind defaults; §15.7).
    - Cost: a change to the fold and to §8.8's creator row.
    - Not taken: (2) `RepoCreate` gaining an owner field for kind mailcfg, valid only from a config writer and checked against a scope row that is not defined yet; (3) the scope row carrying its mailcfg's repo id, which ties a scope's definition to its mail.
    - Raised by the build (the mail-m8-05 track; the build's ledger, QD-1093). It follows owner decision 126's consent rule.

632. **An approved recipient's record is `MailRecipientApproval{alias = 1, recipient = 2, decision = 3, action = 4}` at `rcpt/<h>/<name>`, the name a hash of the pair (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-19).**
    - Question: owner decision 280 puts an approved recipient at `rcpt/<h>/<address>` with a body `MailRecipientApproval{alias, recipient, decision, action}`. No format defined the message, its field numbers, the spelling of the path's last part, since one repo holds several aliases' mail, or which of a multi-recipient decision's recipients a record names, so no record was written and every outside recipient took the gate.
    - Answer: mail.proto gains `message MailRecipientApproval { string alias = 1; string recipient = 2; bytes decision = 3; bytes action = 4; }`, at `rcpt/<h>/<name>` in the mailbox or group repo holding the sending alias's mail, `<name>` the lowercase base32 of SHA-256(alias ‖ 0x00 ‖ recipient), 52 characters, within §11.7's 64-byte names. One repo holding several aliases' mail so keeps one record per (alias, recipient) pair, and its listings say nothing in plaintext. There is one record per recipient a done use names; `decision` is the decision's stored `Signed`, an `ApprovalDecision` on channel device or presence, and `action` the stored `CanonicalAction` of kind `mail_send` listing that recipient. The daemon writes it after the Outcome is done, and readers re-verify both at each use (§15.7's rcpt/ row; §15.10's approved recipients).
    - Cost: a format addition before `mail.proto`'s freeze.
    - Not taken: (2) the recipient address itself as the name, which collides when one repo holds two aliases' mail to one recipient; (3) no records until a customer asks, every outside send taking the gate, the repeat approvals owner decision 280 meant to spare.
    - Raised by the build (the mail-m9-01 track; the build's ledger, QD-1094). It completes owner decision 280.

633. **The key a forward's reverse-alias token is a MAC under is one key per mailbox, sealed in the mailbox's `vault/` family (option 2, the recommendation, 2026-11-19).**
    - Question: §15.10 has a forward go From `r-<token>`, the token a MAC of the original sender "under a vault key". No section named that key's place, bytes or readers. The draft kept a key per device, so two devices forwarding mail from one sender gave that sender two reverse aliases.
    - Answer: one key per mailbox, sealed in the mailbox as a vault file only the owner's consented devices read, as vault entries are (owner decision 440's client rule): `vault/<h>/<reverse-alias-key>` holding a new `MailReverseAliasKey { bytes key = 1; }`, 32 bytes for HMAC-SHA-256, created only if absent by the first forwarding device. Every device of the member then gives one sender one reverse alias. Until it is built, the per-device key holds, which loses only that consistency (§15.7's vault rows; §15.10's forwarding).
    - Cost: a format addition and a reader rule.
    - Not taken: (1) the key per device, as drafted; (3) a key derived from the mailbox's repo key material, a derivation the design does not have and one that ties tokens to key rotation.
    - Raised by the build (the mail-m9-01 track; the build's ledger, QD-1095).

634. **A folded indexer designation carries its IndexerSet header's nonce, `IndexerRow.nonce = 6`, so any reader opens its sealed services (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-19).**
    - Question: an IndexerSet's `sealed_services` is sealed under a context that binds the statement's network id and its header's nonce. The fold copies each standing designation into the projection's rows without the nonce, so a reader could open a designation's services only while it could still read the original entry: a new device that starts at a base, a gate and a long-running reader past the entry opened nothing. The mail component therefore read every designation as unknown: coverage never read `indexer`, the prompt of §15.6 dropped as soon as any designation stood, D-MAIL-15's undesignated-receiver alarm never fired, and a designated `tag:mail` device registered no listener under `team_mail: indexers`.
    - Answer: the projection rows' `IndexerRow` gains `bytes nonce = 6`, the IndexerSet header's 16-byte nonce, copied at the fold, and a reader's view of a designation carries it with the network id the state already holds. Every reader, a new device at a base and a gate included, opens a designation's `sealed_services` under the one seal format every sealed field uses, its context the statement's type, the network id and the nonce. The mail component reads the services so, over its config keys. api-7's row adds the nonce, a random value api already sees in the entry, so it learns nothing new (§7.9's Indexers; §2.8's api-7).
    - Cost: one bytes field in the rows before §7.9's records freeze.
    - Not taken: (2) sealing the services under a context the row keeps, the subject and scope instead of the nonce, which gives one sealed field its own context and lets a sealed value be replayed into a later designation of the same subject and scope; (3) each device keeping the headers it applied and reading a missing one from api's projection, which a new device past the retention point cannot do.
    - Raised by the build (the mail-m8-10 track; the build's ledger, QD-1096). It extends owner decision 330.

635. **MCP's guard refuses the `rcpt/` family on mailboxes and on a general repo that is a group inbox (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-19).**
    - Question: §15.10 says MCP's evs tools refuse `rcpt/`, and no row of the guard did. A record's embedded decision is a person's and verifies at every later use, so an agent that read a record's bytes while it stood could write them back after a person removed it, and the pair would be approved again. The guard's rows go by repo kind; a group's mail lives in a repo of kind general, the kind every ordinary file repo has.
    - Answer: MCP's guard refuses every evs tool, search tool and `ur://evs` resource on the `rcpt/` family, its directory, shards and records, under any ref and omitted from listings, on repos of kind mailbox, as the mailbox's vault row does, and on a repo of kind general only when it is a group inbox. The evs tools tell a group inbox from mailcfg's group records, failing closed, refused, while mailcfg cannot be read for a general repo that has a top-level `rcpt`. Ordinary file repos keep a top-level `rcpt` directory through MCP (§16.6's refused row; D-MCP-17; §15.10).
    - Cost: a mailcfg lookup in the evs tools' target reporting.
    - Not taken: (2) refusing the family on every repo of kind general, which takes a directory name from people's file repos; (3) a signed removal that readers check, a format addition that still leaves agents able to read who is approved.
    - Raised by the build (the mail-m9-01 track; the build's ledger, QD-1097). It follows owner decisions 280 and 632.

636. **An eraser's CLOSURE rewrites a card or repo file only once its standing version no longer holds the erased document; moderation removes a card whole, and an announcement standing on a repo file is not a directory admin's to erase (option 1, the recommendation, 2026-11-19).**
    - Question: §16.11's erasure row has format 2 erase an announcement by a CUT, then a CLOSURE rewriting under fresh keys the index files and the card holding its document. A card is a Signed file under its sidecar's key and a repo file one under a repo admin's; readers refuse any other bytes at those paths, and the eraser, a directory admin's device, holds neither key. The only bytes it can write that readers accept are the standing version's own, which still hold the item when its writer has not dropped it.
    - Answer: a CLOSURE rewrites a card or a repo file only once its standing version no longer holds the erased document. For a revocation, the repo file is rewritten at once, since the owner's marker stands, and each card naming the repo once its writer wrote the run private or left it out; a card that still names the repo has its document CUT from both indexes at once and waits for its writer, caught at each later sync for 30 days. For a directory admin's own erase, a card that still holds the item is removed whole by moderation's removal and its keys erased, the writer's other runs coming back with its next card; an announcement that stands on a repo file is refused, since only the owner's revocation overwrites it. Readers accept a directory admin's push at such a path only when its bytes are the very bytes the copy already accepted there, or else verify as the rightful writer's own Signed under every other rule, whose seq must rise. No format changes. Deferred to the review phase: a reader with nothing accepted at the path cannot hold a replayed earlier Signed to a seq, which is closed by evs marking a CLOSURE's path versions, so readers accept an admin's push only where the cards ref's log shows a CLOSURE at that version whose content hash equals the version before it (§16.11's closure row; DESIGN-DEFER.md's mcp-card-closure-replay-check entry).
    - Cost: a revoked run can stay readable on its card until its daemon next writes, minutes for a running daemon and up to the card's expiry for one that is off; moderation costs the writer's other runs a few minutes of absence; and until the deferred check lands a directory admin can replay a writer's earlier Signed file to a reader that has accepted nothing at its path.
    - Not taken: (2) an admin-signed eraser card that readers accept as erased by an admin, which adds a signed type, a path rule and a reader rule to format 2 and stays the one to design if the owner later wants moderation to keep a writer's other runs or a revoked run gone before its daemon wakes; (3) removal always, which takes down every run of every daemon that named a revoked repo until each writes again.
    - Raised by the build (the record-m10-05 track; the build's ledger, QD-1100).

637. **A schedule fails when it has waited 60 s of real time for a barrier, not 10 s (option 2, the recommendation, 2026-11-19).**
    - Question: §22.8's determinism row failed a schedule that waited over 10 s of real time for a barrier, the barrier driver's default bound. The driver's stall check already tells a blocked holder from a working one within seconds: a holder found blocked loses the baton and its run fails, while a holder found working keeps it until the bound. On a host running five implementation lanes with their builds, a working schedule's compile step ran past the bound twice, at 10.03 s and 10.13 s, and failed a check that passes on a quiet host; each rerun of that check costs 4 to 20 minutes.
    - Answer: the bound is 60 s. It is the barrier driver's default, a settings field, and a test that sets its own bound keeps it. A blocked holder still fails its run at the stall check within seconds, with its trace, so the bound only has to catch a holder that works without end, and a loaded host stops failing schedules that are merely slow (§22.8's determinism row).
    - Cost: a schedule whose holder works on without end, a livelock or a runaway, waits 60 s before it fails with its trace.
    - Not taken: (1) keeping 10 s, with a lane rerunning the check a loaded host fails; (3) 10 s as the bound on time the holder is not found working with 60 s as the cap on all of it, closest to the row's intent but an accounting per reading in the harness every schedule runs on.
    - Raised by the build (the orchestrator, from the search-m10-36 track's report; the build's ledger, QD-1101).

## Owner instructions after the v1 build (2026-11-05)

These are instructions, not design decisions, so they carry no number.

- **SDK gaps: "Design both fixes now."** The two residuals the v1 build left are designed as proposed decisions:
  - stale page Workers when `self_admit` is off (D-TEAM-29, §7.10's `stale` row);
  - revoking a lost device from an app (D-CORE-20 in §5.4 and §5.6, D-SDK-30 in §18.5).

  Their tests are FS-97 and FS-98. Independent checkers reviewed them before the rebuild.
- **Renumbered § references in DESIGN-REVIEW-RESPONSE.md:** keep them.
- **pitch5 (outside DESIGN.md):** update both session lines (:579, :640) to owner decision 170's limit, fix the five footers to cite sidecar/design/reports, and re-render the PDF.
- **Peer review of the v1 additions:** the owner arranges it with their own reviewers, as for the earlier reviews (expect DESIGN-REVIEW4*.md).
